## Background information on the procedure

## 1.1. Type II variation

Pursuant to Article 16 of Commission Regulation (EC) No 1234/2008, Janssen-Cilag International NV submitted to the European Medicines Agency on 6 January 2026 an application for a variation.

The following changes were proposed:

| Variation(s) requested   | Variation(s) requested                                                              | Type              |
|--------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|
| C.I.6.a                  | C.I.6.a Addition of a new therapeutic indication or modification of an approved one | Variation type II |

Extension of indication to include in combination with daratumumab treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least one prior therapy for TECVAYLI, based on interim analysis data from the pivotal study MajesTEC-3 (64007957MMY3001). This is an on-going multicentre, randomised, open-label, Phase 3 study to determine whether adding teclistamab to daratumumab (Tec-Dara) is more efficacious than adding pomalidomide/dexamethasone (DPd) or bortezomib/dexamethasone (DVd) to daratumumab in participants with multiple myeloma who previously received 1 to 3 prior line(s) of therapy. As a consequence, sections 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.7, 4.8, 5.1, 5.2 and 6.6 of the SmPC are updated. The Package Leaflet is updated accordingly. References to the conditional MA have been removed throughout the document. Additionally, the MAH took the opportunity to update the latest renewal date in section 9 of the SmPC, the list of local representatives in the Package Leaflet and made editorial changes throughout. An updated RMP version 6.1 was submitted. As part of the application, the MAH is requesting a 1-year extension of the market protection.

## Information on paediatric requirements

Pursuant to Article 8 of Regulation (EC) No 1901/2006, the application included an EMA Decision(s) P/0044/2020 on the granting of a (product-specific) waiver.

## Information relating to orphan market exclusivity

## Similarity

Pursuant to Article 8 of Regulation (EC) No. 141/2000 and Article 3 of Commission Regulation (EC) No 847/2000, the application included a critical report addressing the possible similarity with authorised orphan medicinal products.

## Information on paediatric requirements

Not applicable

## Information relating to orphan market exclusivity

Not applicable

## MAH request for additional market protection

The MAH requested consideration of its application in accordance with Article 14(11) of Regulation (EC) 726/2004 - one year of market protection for a new indication bringing significant clinical benefit versus existing therapies.

## Scientific advice

The MAH received protocol assistance from the CHMP on 25 February 2021(EMA/SA/0000050045) and 11 November 2021 (EMA/SA/0000069104). The protocol assistance pertained to non-clinical and clinical aspects of the dossier.

## 2. Scientific discussion

## 2.1. Introduction

## 2.1.1. Problem statement

## Disease or condition

The MAH submitted an extension of indication application to add a new indication of teclistamab solution  for  injection  in  combination  with  daratumumab  for  the  treatment  of  adult  patients  with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least one prior therapy.

This application is largely based upon the interim progression free survival (PFS) results of the pivotal phase 3 study MajesTEC-3 (64007957MMY3001).

## Epidemiology

Multiple myeloma (MM) is a rare and incurable plasma cell neoplasm which typically affects adults mostly over 60 years of age. The median age at diagnosis is 65-70 years; MM is very rare in patients younger than 40 years old (2% of cases).

MM accounts for 1%-1.8% of all cancers and is the second most common haematological malignancy (after non-Hodgkin's lymphoma [NHL]) with an estimated incidence in Europe of 4.5-6/100 000/year, with  approximately  176,404  new  MM  cases  and  117,077  deaths  due  to  MM  anticipated  in  2020 worldwide (The Global Cancer Observatory 2020).

Multiple Myeloma is characterized by the increased proliferation of malignant monoclonal plasma cells in the bone marrow, with the subsequent bone marrow failure due to replacement of normal bone marrow haematopoiesis, the over-production of monoclonal immunoglobulins (M-protein, either intact immunoglobulins and/or free light chains [FLC]) which could be detected in the serum or urine, and finally the presence of systemic symptoms named as CRAB (hyperCalcemia, Renal impairment, Anaemia and Bone lesions). Increased susceptibility to infections (immunoparesis) and neurological complications are also present (Palumbo 2011).

Based on karyotype, MM is classified as non-hyperdiploid and hyperdiploid, with the latter accounting for 50% to 60% of cases and characterized by trisomies in odd-numbered chromosomes. MM has a heterogeneous  progression  pathway,  with  multiple  relapses  over  time,  whereby  several  MM  cell subclones coexist at baseline and compete for dominance over time, leading to the evolution of drugresistance clones [Laubach, 2014].

Drug resistance to prior regimens in patients with relapsed/refractory (RR) MM is due to continuous changes in the disease biology, in which a higher proportion of malignant cells are expressing a more aggressive, highly proliferative phenotype over time (Anderson, 2008).

## Biologic features, aetiology and pathogenesis

MM  is  characterised  by  marrow  plasmacytomas  (plasma  cell  tumours)  and  overproduction  of monoclonal immunoglobulins (IgG, IgA, IgD or IgE) or Bence-Jones protein (monoclonal K or h light

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

chains), while the production of normal immunoglobulin is impaired. The cause of a myeloma cell's failure to differentiate is unknown.

MM  represents  the  far  end  of  the  spectrum  of  B  cell-derived  neoplasms.  It  is  the  neoplastic counterpart  of  terminally  differentiated  immunoglobulin-producing,  long-lived  plasma  cells  (PCs). Long-lived PCs are a subset of PCs characterized by long-term (months to years) survival within the bone marrow (BM) and thought to be key for immunologic memory. Based on karyotype, MM is classified as nonhyperdiploid and hyper diploid, with the latter accounting for 50% to 60% of cases and characterized by trisomies in odd chromosomes.

Tumour cells do not grow isolated from their surroundings, but they rather establish close ties with the  microenvironment  important  for  tumour  survival  and  progression.  Unlike  solid  malignancies, where the sites of primary disease and metastases are typically distinct, MM is characterised by widespread cancer involvement of multiple sites within the same microenvironment: the BM. The BM niche, therefore, acquires primary interest as a pathogenic factor in MM.

## Clinical presentation, diagnosis and stage/prognosis

The clinical features of MM are varied and can arise from the effects of the tumour itself, or the toxicity of the tumour products, or the host's own immune response.

The most common symptoms include persistent skeletal pain (especially pain in the back or thorax), pathological  fractures  and  vertebral  collapse,  anaemia,  renal  impairment,  hypercalcaemia  and recurrent or persistent bacterial infections. Approximately 20% of patients are asymptomatic at the time of diagnosis.

The most common criteria used in diagnosis of symptomatic MM is the presence of neoplastic plasma cells  comprising  greater  than  10%  of  BM  cells  or  presence  of  a  plasmacytoma;  paraprotein  (Mprotein) in the serum and/or urine; and evidence of related organ or tissue impairment due to plasma cell disorder.

The International Staging System (ISS) is used for prognosis. It was revised by the International Myeloma Working Group (IMWG) including cytogenetics by fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) and lactate dehydrogenase (LD, to the Revised International Staging System for Multiple Myeloma, R-ISS), which is now widely accepted. At the time of diagnosis, patients are typically categorized according to R-ISS, their age, comorbidity and their suitability for intensive treatment.

Despite advances in therapy, MM remains incurable. All patients eventually relapse and with each successive  relapse,  the  chance  of  response  and  duration  of  response  typically  decreases  and ultimately the disease becomes refractory and results in cumulative end organ damage (e.g., renal damage, cytopenia, infections  and  bone  weakening).  Patients  with  relapsed  disease  can  achieve responses to subsequent anti-myeloma regimens, the duration of response typically decreases with successive relapses until disease becomes resistant to all different options.

## Management

Currently  there  are  several  different  classes  of  approved  agents  for  MM,  including  proteasome inhibitors (PIs), immunomodulatory agents, mAbs targeting a range of antigens, steroids, alkylators, and  selective  inhibitors  of  nuclear  export,  which  can  be  combined  in  doublet,  triplet,  or  even quadruplet regimens and used with or without high-dose chemotherapy rescued by autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT) (Moreau, 2021).

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Different combination regimens have been studied in clinical trials in these settings (Mikhael, 2019; Dimopoulos, 2021; Moreau, 2021; NCCN, 2022). Despite this, there remains a need for expanding the therapeutic armamentarium with more treatment options, especially for the increasing number of patients who have received 1 or more prior line/s of therapy and are exposed or refractory to standard agents like PIs, lenalidomide, and anti-CD38 antibodies.

Further  advanced  targeted  therapies  like  chimeric  antigen  receptor-T  cell  (CAR-T)  and  bispecific antibody-based therapies have also been added to the therapeutic armamentarium in recent years. CAR-T therapies are now approved as second- and third-line treatments. Several bispecifics are currently approved for patients who have received 4 or more lines of therapy.

Treatment of RRMM is complex, as it has to be individualised based on several patient-related (e.g., age,  ECOG performance status, comorbidities, patient preference), treatment-related (e.g., prior exposure and refractoriness to specific therapies, depth and duration of response [DoR] to prior therapies, toxicity from prior therapies), and disease related factors (e.g., cytogenetic risk status, presence of extramedullary disease [EMD], relapse characteristics [biochemical vs. clinical]).

## 2.1.2. About the product

Teclistamab  (INN)  is  a  humanised  immunoglobulin  G4  (IgG4)  bispecific  antibody  against  B-cell maturation antigen (BCMA) and cluster of differentiation 3 (CD3) receptors. The BCMA targeting arm engages a BCMA presenting malignant B cell, followed by the engagement of an activated T cell by the CD3 binding arm, resulting in malignant cell death due to cell lysis mediated by secreted perforin and various granzymes stored in the secretory vesicles of cytotoxic T cells.

Teclistamab is approved as monotherapy in subjects with relapsed and refractory multiple myeloma who  have  received  at  least  three  prior  therapies,  including  an  immunomodulatory  agent,  a proteasome inhibitor, and an anti-CD38 antibody and have demonstrated disease progression on the last therapy.

## 2.1.3. The development programme/compliance with CHMP guidance/scientific advice

The applicant sought scientific advice from CHMP on the design of the pivotal study of this application.

The applicant's initial plan was to randomise subjects to receive either Tec-DP (teclistamab and daratumumab in combination with pomalidomide) or daratumumab in combination with pomalidomide and dexamethasone (DPd). However, further to the product's PRIME designation, the applicant presented a revised study plan for MajesTEC-3. The revised plan included a comparator arm of physician's choice (DPd or daratumumab in combination with bortezomib and dexamethasone (DVd)). In line with this change, a combination of teclistamab with daratumumab (Tec-Dara) was planned in the experimental arm instead of combination with daratumumab and pomalidomide. This revised plan was confirmed as acceptable by the CHMP in the follow-up scientific advice in November 2021 (EMA/SA/0000069104).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.2. Non-clinical aspects

No new non-clinical data have been submitted in this application, which was considered acceptable by the CHMP.

## 2.2.1. Ecotoxicity/environmental risk assessment

Teclistamab  is  a  monoclonal  antibody  and  is  consequently  classified  as  a  naturally  occurring substance.  In  accordance  with  the  Guideline  on  the  Environmental  Risk  Assessment  (ERA)  of Medicinal Products for Human Use (EMEA/CHMP/SWP/4447/00 Revision 1), the MAH submitted a justification for not submitting ERA studies and considered that teclistamab is unlikely to pose a risk to the environment.

## 2.2.2. Conclusion on the non-clinical aspects

The CHMP agreed with the MAH's justification for not submitting ERA studies as teclistamab is not expected to pose a risk to the environment.

## 2.3. Clinical aspects

## 2.3.1. Introduction

## GCP

The clinical trials were performed in accordance with GCP as claimed by the MAH.

The MAH has provided a statement to the effect that clinical trials conducted outside the community were carried out in accordance with the ethical standards of Directive 2001/20/EC.

## 2.3.2. Pharmacokinetics

The applicant has provided pharmacokinetic data from two studies 64007957MMY3001 (MajesTEC3) and 64407564MMY1002 (TriMM-2). In addition, the applicant provided a population pharmacokinetics and  exposure-response report.

A new enhanced method for the determination of antidrug antibodies (ADAs) was developed. 100 ng/ml anti-BCMA Arm ADA BCMB164 and anti-JNJ-64007957 specific ADA could be detected up to 250 µg/ml teclistamab. The assay was fully validated.

Other bioanalytical assays for teclistamab and daratumumab were the same as in the original MAA. Specificity testing of daratumumab assays in the presence of teclistamab was performed and found to have no effect on the PK, ADA, and NAb assays. All validation reports and bioanalytical reports were provided.

## PK results from the non-RP2D weight-based cohorts

## PK results from the fixed dose cohorts

The mean predose concentrations in 2-tiered fixed dose cohorts (weight ≤ 60.0 kg: 100 mg weekly for Cycles 1-2, 200 mg Q2W for Cycles 3-6, Q4W for Cycles 7+ [N = 11]; weight &gt;60.0 kg: 150 mg weekly for Cycles 1-2 and 300 mg Q2W for Cycles 3-6, Q4W for Cycles 7+ [N = 20]) were slightly higher when compared with those in the RP2D cohorts, with overlapping concentration ranges. Based on these limited data, the predose teclistamab concentrations (at C2D1 or C3D1) seemed comparable in participants with or without Grade 5 infections.

## Drug-drug interactions related to release of cytokines

PBPK  modelling  and  simulation  based  on  observed  elevation  of  IL-6  levels  in  MajesTEC-1  was conducted previously to assess the risk of DDIs with teclistamab when administered as monotherapy

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

and inform dose recommendations. The MAH considers that given similar incidence, severity, and duration of CRS observed in monotherapy and in the Tec-Dara combination, no additional analyses were required.

## Population PK analysis

## Objectives

The objectives of the popPK analysis were to:

- Confirm whether  the existing teclistamab  monotherapy  popPK  model  developed  in participants  with  RRMM  is  adequate  to  describe  teclistamab  PK  in  combination  with daratumumab obtained in TriMM-2 and MajesTEC-3.
- Evaluate the teclistamab exposure metrics in different subpopulations.
- Derive teclistamab exposure metrics based on the popPK model, which will be used as input for the E-R analyses.

## Model development

The pivotal MajesTec-3 and supporting TriMM-2 study were pooled for the popPK analysis.

The existing popPK model (see EPAR for initial marketing authorisation for Tecvayli) was built based on data from MajesTEC-1 in participants  with  RRMM receiving  teclistamab  as  monotherapy.  The popPK  model  comprised  a  2-compartment  model  with  first-order  absorption  and  parallel  timeindependent and time-dependent elimination pathways. The model is parameterised in terms of CL1, CL2, V1, KDES, Q, V2, Ka, and F. The parameters and covariate-PK relationships are shown in Table 6 .

Table 6. Parameter estimates for the existing teclistamab PopPK model based on data from MajesTEC-1 in participants with RRMM receiving teclistamab as monotherapy

| Parameters, unit    | Estimate    | RSE (%)        |   IIV (%CV) |   RSE (%) |   Shrinkage (%) |
|---------------------|-------------|----------------|-------------|-----------|-----------------|
| CL₁ (L/day)³        | 0.449 0.704 | 8.87 21.8 7.83 |        53.6 |      14.3 |            14.4 |
| BWT on CL1          |             |                |             |           |                 |
| ISS=II on CL₁       | 1.31        |                |             |           |                 |

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The Goodness of fit (GOF) plots and Prediction-Corrected Visual Predictive Checks (pcVPC) were used as external evaluation methods. GOF plots were generated via (Maximum a Posteriori) MAP approach by  using  the  popPK  model  parameters  ( Table  6 )  as  prior  information.  GOF  plots  assessed  the concordance  between  the  popPK  model  predictions  and  the  observed  data  and  various  plots  of residuals.  In  addition,  the  pcVPC  was  performed  and  the  5th,  50th,  and  95th  percentiles  of  the observed data over time bins were compared with the corresponding 95% CIs generated from 500 simulated  replicates  by  using  the  existing  popPK  model  parameters.  Uncertainty  in  parameter estimates was excluded in these simulations. The pcVPC were stratified by study (i.e., TriMM-2 and MajesTEC-3).

## Data

Following exclusion of samples below quantification limits or which were not analysed, the popPK analysis  was  conducted  using  a  total  of  2515  teclistamab  PK  samples  from  385  PK-evaluable participants:  1490  samples  from  275  participants  in  MajesTEC-3  and  1025  samples  from  110 participants in TriMM-2. The PK-evaluable participants were defined as participants who received at least 1 dose of teclistamab and had at least 1 serum teclistamab concentration value above LLOQ of 0.0005 μg/mL after teclistamab dose administration.

Based on the GOF plots ( Figure 3 ), the observed versus the population- and individual-predicted teclistamab concentrations had a random scatter around the identity line, indicating the absence of significant bias.

In addition, the MAH concluded that the pcVPC result ( Figure 4 ) showed that the existing popPK model adequately describes the central tendency and magnitude of variability of the teclistamab concentration-time profiles from TriMM-2 and MajesTEC-3 studies. Accordingly, the MAH concluded that the prior popPK model, which was developed in participants with RRMM receiving teclistamab as monotherapy, successfully characterized the PK of teclistamab in combination with daratumumab indicating that daratumumab has no impact on teclistamab PK.

## Subgroup Analysis of the Exposure Metrics in MajesTEC - 3

Only participants who received at least 1 dose of teclistamab in MajesTEC-3 were included in this analysis.  Two  specific  exposure  metrics  were  selected  to  characterise:  early-treatment  exposure (predicted average concentration of the first treatment dose, Cave,1stdose) and long-term treatment exposure (predicted trough concentration after the first biweekly treatment dose, Ctrough,Q2W). These exposure metrics were derived for each participant with measurable serum concentrations based on the  nominal  teclistamab  dose  schedule  of  the  MajesTEC-3:  1.5  mg/kg  SC  QW  for  Cycles  1  to  2 (preceded by step-up doses of 0.06 mg/kg and 0.3 mg/kg), followed by 3.0 mg/kg SC Q2W for Cycles 3 to 6, and then 3.0 mg/kg SC Q4W from Cycle 7 onward. These exposure metrics were used to evaluate the impact of covariates on PK and inform predefined subgroup analyses, performed via forest plots.

Forest plots were created for the comparison of teclistamab exposures in different subpopulations of interest and were categorised in the case of continuous covariates using the cutoffs described in MajesTEC-1 and MajesTEC-3 analysis plan ( Figure 5 ).

Notes: This analysis assumed that all the participants in study MajesTEC-3 included in the popPK analysis

The subgroup analysis of Cave,1stdose, showed  that the statistically significant PK-covariate relationships  described  in Table 6 such  as  body  weight  and  ISS  (ISS=I,  II,  and  III)  and  other covariates of interest were deemed not to be clinically meaningful. The point estimate of the GMR for Cave,1stdose based on each covariate effect remained within the 80 to 125% interval relative to the reference group as indicated in Figure 5 except for type of myeloma (IgG versus non-IgG). The model-predicted Cave,1stdose was approximately 20% lower in participants with IgG type of myeloma compared with those with non-IgG type of myeloma.

For the long-term treatment exposure (Ctrough,Q2W), the GMR by covariate remained within 80 to 125% of the reference interval for all covariates except type of myeloma and Chinese ethnicity (data not shown). The model-predicted Ctrough,Q2W was approximately 24% lower in participants with IgG type of  myeloma compared with those with non-IgG type of myeloma. This finding is consistent with Cave,1stdose suggesting that the IgG impact on teclistamab PK is similar for both early and long-term treatment  durations.  The  GMR  for  Ctrough,Q2W  between  Chinese  and  the  other  participants  was estimated to be 78.7, marginally outside the prespecified 80%-125% reference interval. In contrast, Cave,1stdose provided a GMR of 92.7 which fell within the expected reference range. Given the small number of Chinese subjects (n=26), the ethnicity-related PK effect was considered unlikely to be clinically meaningful. No dose adjustment was recommended based on any of these factors.

## 2.3.3. Pharmacodynamics

No new data on clinical pharmacodynamics were submitted.

## 2.3.4. PK/PD modelling

A  total  of  273  participants  who  received  at  least  1  administration  of  study  treatment  and  had evaluable  teclistamab  exposure  metrics  (ie,  Cmax,1stdose)  in  Tec-Dara  arm  together  with  290 participants in DPd/DVd who received at least 1 administration of study treatment were included in the exposure-efficacy and safety datasets.

The objectives of the Exposure Response (E-R) analyses were to:

Confirm  and  supplement  the  evidence  of  efficacy  and  safety  of  teclistamab  dosing  regimen  in participants who received teclistamab in combination with daratumumab in MajesTEC-3 by:

- o Exploring  the  E-R  relationship  between  teclistamab  exposure  and  the  primary  efficacy endpoint PFS.
- o Exploring the E-R relationship between teclistamab exposure and safety endpoints including anaemia, neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and infections.

## Exposure-response analysis on efficacy

Progression-free survival (PFS) was the efficacy endpoint used in these analyses. The investigated covariates  were  overall  balanced  between  the  treatment  arms  and  the  quartiles  of  exposure (Cave,1stdose) from the Tec-Dara arm. The type of myeloma and sBCMA showed slight imbalance across the quartiles of exposure in Tec-Dara arm. The IgG-type participants were more frequently observed

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

in the lowest exposure quartile (Q1; 67.7%) than in the highest exposure quartile (Q4; 37.5%), indicating that IgG-type is associated with lower teclistamab exposure. As expected, opposite finding was observed for the non-IgG type participants were the proportion of these participants increased with higher teclistamab exposure Q1 (29.0%) to Q4 (72.1%).

Within  the  exposure  range  achieved  at  the  proposed  dosing  regimen,  no  statistically  significant exposure-PFS relationship was observed for teclistamab, when analysed by quartiles of exposure or as a continuous variable in the univariate Cox regression model using exposure metric Cave,1stdose ( Table 7 and Figure 6 ).

## Exposure-response analysis on safety

Given the high correlation (r&gt;0.80) between Cmax,1stdose and Cmax,4doses and considering that certain treatment emergent adverse events (TEAE) such as infections predominantly occur during the initial dosing period, Cmax,1stdose was selected to perform the exposure-safety analysis as it represents an early  indicator  of  systemic  exposure.  Bar  plots  of  selected  TEAE  rates  (Grade ≥ 3)  stratified  by teclistamab exposure quantile based on Cmax,1stdose are presented in Figure 7 .

The total number of deaths due to fatal infections (Grade 5) in MajesTEC-3 was 17, with a higher incidence in the Tec-Dara arm (Tec-Dara: 13; DPd/DVd: 4). Of the 13 fatal infections in the TecDara group, 12 occurred within the first 6 months of treatment initiation. The distribution of these events was analysed in relation to the Tec-Dara exposure metric (Cmax,1stdose) and the DPd/DVd arm ( Table 9 ). Among the 13 participants, one could not have their Cmax,1stdose exposure determined, as they only received the step-up dose and therefore was not included in the analysis.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.3.5. Discussion on clinical pharmacology

## Comparison  of  observed  PK  between  monotherapy  of  teclistamab  and  teclistamab  in combination with daratumumab

## Teclistamab observed PK across studies

PK parameters (Cmax, AUCtau) following the first treatment dose of 1.5 mg/kg in Tec-Dara (TriMM-2) was shown to be comparable to those estimated after the first treatment dose of 1.5 mg/kg in monotherapy (MajesTEC-1). However, the number of evaluated subjects was small (n=4 in TriMM2, n=40 in MajesTEC-1).

The MAH compared also the pre-dose concentrations of the first and second 1.5 mg/kg dose of teclistamab between the teclistamab monotherapy study (MajesTEC-1) and the Tec-Dara combination (MajesTEC-3 and TriMM-2). These values were generally comparable. Due to differences in dosing regimens after Cycle 3, cycle durations, and sampling time points between the Tec-Dara combination studies (MajesTEC-3 and TriMM-2) and teclistamab monotherapy study (MajesTEC-1), comparison of concentrations at later time points were not performed. Therefore, the MAH used population PK model to justify similarities in PK of teclistamab in monotherapy and in combination with daratumumab. This is discussed under Population PK analysis section.

## Daratumumab observed PK within 64007957MMY3001 (MajesTEC - 3)

A total of 268 participants in the Tec-Dara group and 280 participants in the control group were used to  compare  daratumumab  PK  with  or  without  teclistamab.  Observed  predose  concentrations  of daratumumab with or without teclistamab were generally within the same range.

## Population PK analysis

The MAH has used existing teclistamab monotherapy popPK model to conduct external validation to verify the model predictive performance with the current clinical PK data from combination therapy trials. The existing popPK model was built based on data from MajesTEC-1 in participants with RRMM receiving teclistamab as monotherapy. The population PK data set used now for external validation included total of 2515 teclistamab PK samples from 385 PK-evaluable participants: 1490 samples from 275 participants in MajesTEC-3 and 1025 samples from 110 participants in TriMM-2. Majority of PK samples were taken predose at different cycles. As the amount of BQL records postdose was very low (0.85% of all samples) they were excluded from the popPK analysis (M1 method). This is acceptable.

GOF plots and pcVPC were used as external evaluation methods. GOF plots were generated via MAP approach by using the existing popPK model parameters as prior information. GOF plots assessed the concordance between the popPK model predictions and the observed data and various plots of residuals. This approach is considered reasonable.

The  observed  versus  the  population-  and  individual-predicted  teclistamab  concentrations  had  a random scatter around the identity line, indicating the absence of significant bias. The distribution of IWRES as function of the individual predictions showed slightly decreasing trend. The distribution of CWRES as function of time showed decreasing trend and CWRES values below zero after 200 days since first dose. These trends might indicate some difference in PK profiles of monotherapy compared to  combination  therapy  with  daratumumab  following  long-term  use  (&gt;6  months).  However,  this difference  is  unlikely  to  be  clinically  meaningful  considering  the  observed  efficacy  and  safety  of monotherapy and combination therapy with daratumumab.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In addition, the pcVPC was performed and the 5th, 50th, and 95th percentiles of the observed data over  time  bins  were  compared  with  the  corresponding  95%  CIs  generated  from  500  simulated replicates by using the existing popPK model parameters. Uncertainty in parameter estimates was excluded in these simulations. The pcVPC were stratified by study (ie, TriMM-2 and MajesTEC-3).

## Subgroup Analysis of the Exposure Metrics in MajesTEC - 3

Two specific exposure metrics were selected to characterize: early-treatment exposure (Cave,1stdose) and long-term treatment exposure (Ctrough,Q2W). These exposure metrics were used to evaluate the impact of covariates on PK and inform predefined subgroup analyses, performed via forest plots. If the  point  estimate  of  the  GMR  for  exposure  (Cave,1stdose  or  Ctrough,Q2W)  based  on  covariate  effect remained within the 80 to 125% interval relative to the reference group, it was considered that covariate effect was not clinically meaningful. The choice of exposure metrics and subgroup analysis method can be considered acceptable.

The subgroup analysis of Cave,1stdose and Ctrough,Q2W showed that only covariate that was consistently outside the 80 to 125% interval relative to the reference group was type of myeloma (IgG versus non-IgG). This finding is consistent with observations from MajesTEC-1, which were assessed in the initial MAA. The effect of type of myeloma (IgG versus non-IgG) was further evaluated in the E-R analysis, which indicated no need for dose adjustment based on type of myeloma.

In addition, considering subgroup analysis of patients with hepatic impairment, mild (n=38) and moderate  (n=3)  were  pooled  together  and  compared  to  patients  with  normal  function.  This  is considered acceptable approach, and results indicate that mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt;1 to 1.5 times upper limit of normal (ULN) and any aspartate aminotransferase (AST), or total bilirubin ≤ULN and AST&gt;ULN) did not significantly influence the pharmacokinetics of teclistamab . However, since the number of patients with moderate hepatic impairment is so low, it is not possible to make definite conclusions on its effect on exposure.

For subgroup analysis of renal impairment, moderate (n=48) was pooled with severe (n=1). Again, this is an acceptable approach, but it is not possible to make definite conclusions for the effect of severe renal impairment on exposure. See discussion related to the product information below.

Overall, no dose adjustment is recommended based on any of these factors.

## Exposure-efficacy analysis in 64007957MMY3001 (MajesTEC-3)

A total of 273 participants in Tec-Dara arm together with 290 participants in DPd/DVd arm were included in the exposure-efficacy dataset. Nearly all of the investigated covariates were well balanced between the treatment arms and the quartiles of exposure (Cave,1stdose) from the Tec-Dara arm. The IgG-type participants were more frequently observed in the lowest exposure quartile than in the highest exposure quartile and for the non-IgG type participants the opposite was observed, i.e. their proportion was greatest in the highest teclistamab exposure quartile and smallest in in the lowest exposure quartile. This trend is consistent with the population PK model where the type of myeloma is a covariate on clearance.

The  exposure  metrics  selected  to  the  exposure-efficacy  analyses  were:  Cave,1stdose,  Ctrough,4doses, Ctrough,Q2W and Ctrough,Q4W. The efficacy endpoint was PFS. These exposure metrics and the efficacy endpoint are acceptable for the exposure-efficacy analyses.

The exposure-PFS relationship for teclistamab was analysed both by quartiles of exposure or as a continuous  variable  in  the  univariate  Cox  regression  model  using  exposure  metrics  Cave,1stdose, Ctrough,4doses, Ctrough,Q2W, and Ctrough,Q4W. No statistically significant exposure-PFS relationship was seen for the exposure range of teclistamab at the proposed dosing regimen in these analyses.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Also, the inclusion of type of myeloma (IgG versus non-IgG) did not have a statistically significant effect on PFS and was therefore not considered clinically meaningful. Although teclistamab exposures for Cave,1stdose  and  Ctrough,Q2W  were  lower in participants with IgG type of myeloma compared with those with non-IgG type of myeloma, the multivariate Cox regression analysis provided evidence that both types of participants achieve similar PFS with the MajesTEC-3 dosing regimen. The results indicate no need for dose adjustment based on type of myeloma.

## Exposure-safety analysis in 64007957MMY3001 (MajesTEC-3)

A total of 273 participants in Tec-Dara arm together with 290 participants in DPd/DVd were included in the exposure-safety dataset. Exposure-safety was assessed using bar plots of selected TEAE rates (Grade ≥ 3)  stratified  by  teclistamab  exposure  quantile  based  on  Cmax,1stdose.  This  method  can  be considered  acceptable.  It  is  seen  that  no  increase  in  TEAE  rates  was  observed  with  increasing teclistamab exposure (Cmax,1stdose) for anaemia, neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and infections within the studied teclistamab concentration range.

The  higher  number  of  deaths  due  to  fatal  infections  (Grade  5)  was  higher  in  the  Tec-Dara  arm compared to DPd/DVd arm (Tec-Dara: 13; DPd/DVd: 4). Of the 13 fatal infections in the Tec-Dara group, 12 occurred within the first 6 months of treatment initiation. The distribution of these events was analysed in relation to the Tec-Dara exposure metric (Cmax,1stdose) and the DPd/DVd arm. Among the 13 participants, one could not have their Cmax,1stdose exposure determined, as they only received the step-up dose and therefore was not included in the analysis. It is acknowledged that given the limited  sample  size,  no  association  between  Cmax,1stdose  and  Grade  5  fatal  infections  (n=12)  was observed in the Tec-Dara arm.

## 2.3.6. Conclusions on clinical pharmacology

Pharmacokinetics of teclistamab in combination with daratumumab were evaluated using observed pharmacokinetic data from two studies 64007957MMY3001 (MajesTEC-3) and 64407564MMY1002 (TriMM-2) and population pharmacokinetic analysis with a model developed previously. The analyses conducted  suggest  that  pharmacokinetics  of  teclistamab  is  similar  between  monotherapy  and combination therapy with daratumumab and that no dose adjustment based e.g. on age, gender, and  ethnicity  are  needed.  Within  the  exposure  range  of  MajesTEC-3  study,  no  exposure-PFS  or exposure-safety relationship was evident.

## 2.4. Clinical efficacy

## 2.4.1. Main study

MajesTEC-3  (64007957MMY3001): A  phase  3  randomized  study  comparing  teclistamab  in combination  with  subcutaneous  daratumumab  (Tec-Dara)  versus  subcutaneous  daratumumab, pomalidomide,  and  dexamethasone  (DPd)  or  subcutaneous  daratumumab,  bortezomib,  and dexamethasone (DVd) in subjects with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma.

## Methods

A diagrammatic representation of the study design is presented in Figure 9.

Figure 9 . Schematic Overview of MajesTEC-3 Study

<!-- image -->

Participants received study treatment until confirmed progressive disease, death, intolerable toxicity, withdrawal of consent, or end of the study, whichever occurred first.

If study treatment is discontinued prior to the onset of disease progression per IMWG criteria, disease evaluation  should  continue  to  be  performed  until  confirmed  disease  progression,  death,  start  of subsequent antimyeloma therapy (unless disease progression is not confirmed prior to start of such therapy), withdrawal of consent, or end of the study, whichever occurs first.

## Study participants

## Inclusion Criteria (selection)

1. ≥ 18 years of age (or the legal age of consent in the jurisdiction in which the study is taking place) at the time of informed consent.
2. Documented multiple myeloma as defined by the criteria below:
- a. Multiple myeloma diagnosis according to the IMWG diagnostic criteria
- b. Measurable disease at screening as defined by any of the following:
- Serum M-protein level ≥ 0.5 g/dL (central laboratory); or
- Urine M-protein level ≥ 200 mg/24 hours (central laboratory); or
- Serum immunoglobulin free light chain ≥ 10 mg/dL (central laboratory) and abnormal serum immunoglobulin kappa lambda free light chain ratio.
3. Received 1 to 3 prior line(s) of antimyeloma therapy (Appendix 6) including a PI and lenalidomide.

a.  Participants  who  have  received  only  1  line  of  prior  line  of  antimyeloma  therapy  must  be lenalidomide  refractory  (i.e.,  have  demonstrated  stable  disease  or  progressive  disease  by  IMWG criteria during treatment or within 60 days of completion of lenalidomide-containing regimen). Stable disease or progression on or within 60 days of the last dose of lenalidomide given as maintenance will meet this criterion.

4. Documented evidence of progressive disease
5.  Have  an  ECOG  performance  status  score  of  0,  1,  or  2  at  screening  and  prior  to  the  start  of administration of study treatment
6. Adequate clinical laboratory values and organ function during the Screening Phase.
7. Female subjects must not be pregnant and avoid pregnancy through adequate means.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Exclusion Criteria (selection)

1. Contraindications or life-threatening allergies, hypersensitivity, or intolerance to any study drug or its excipients. Additional exclusion criteria pertaining to specific study drugs include:

a. A participant is not eligible to receive DPd as control therapy if any of the following are present:

- Contraindications or life-threatening allergies, hypersensitivity, or intolerance to pomalidomide (intolerance defined as prior therapy discontinued due to any AE related to pomalidomide)
- Disease that is considered refractory to pomalidomide per IMWG criteria

b. A participant is not eligible to receive DVd as control therapy if any of the following are present:

- Contraindications or life-threatening allergies, hypersensitivity, or intolerance to bortezomib (intolerance defined as prior therapy discontinued due to any AE related to bortezomib)
- Grade 1 peripheral neuropathy with pain or Grade ≥ 2 peripheral neuropathy
- Disease that is considered refractory to bortezomib per IMWG
- Received a strong CYP3A4 inducer within 5 half-lives prior to randomisation

c.  A  participant  is  not  eligible  for  this  study  if  they  are  refractory  to  both  pomalidomide  and bortezomib.

2. Received any prior BCMA-directed therapy.

3. Has disease that is considered refractory to an anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody per IMWG criteria

4.  Received  the  following  prior  antimyeloma  therapy,  in  the  specified  time  frame  prior  to randomisation:

a. Targeted therapy, epigenetic therapy, or treatment with an investigational drug or an invasive investigational medical device within 21 days or ≥ 5 half-lives, whichever is less

- b. Investigational vaccine within 4 weeks
- c. Monoclonal antibody therapy within 21 days
- d. Cytotoxic therapy within 21 days
- e. PI therapy within 14 days
- f. IMiD agent therapy within 14 days

g. Radiotherapy within 14 days or focal radiation within 7 days

h. Gene-modified adoptive cell therapy (e.g., chimeric antigen receptor modified T cells, NK cells) within 3 months.

5. Stem cell transplant:

a. An allogeneic stem cell transplant within 6 months before randomisation. Participants who received an allogeneic transplant must be off all immunosuppressive medications for ≥ 42 days without signs of graft-versus-host disease before randomisation.

b. An autologous stem cell transplant within 12 weeks before randomisation.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

6. Myelodysplastic syndrome or active malignancies (i.e., progressing or requiring treatment change in the last 24 months) other than relapsed/refractory multiple myeloma. The only allowed exceptions are:

a.  Non-muscle  invasive  bladder  cancer  treated  within  the  last  24  months  that  is  considered completely cured

b. Skin cancer (non-melanoma or melanoma) treated within the last 24 months that is considered completely cured

c. Noninvasive cervical cancer treated within the last 24 months that is considered completely cured

d. Localized prostate cancer (N0M0):

- With  a  Gleason  score  of ≤ 6,  treated  within  the  last  24  months,  or  untreated  and  under surveillance
- With a Gleason score of 3+4 that has been treated &gt;6 months prior to full study screening and considered to have a very low risk of recurrence, or
- History  of  localized  prostate  cancer  and  receiving  androgen  deprivation  therapy  and considered to have a very low risk of recurrence.

e. Breast cancer: adequately treated lobular carcinoma in situ or ductal carcinoma in situ, or history of localized breast cancer and receiving antihormonal agents and considered to have a very low risk of recurrence

f. Other malignancy that is considered cured with minimal risk of recurrence.

9.  Plasma  cell  leukemia  at  the  time  of  screening,  Waldenstrom ' s  macroglobulinemia,  POEMS syndrome  (polyneuropathy,  organomegaly, endocrinopathy,  M-protein,  and  skin  changes),  or primary amyloid light chain amyloidosis.

10. CNS involvement or clinical signs of meningeal involvement of multiple myeloma.

11. Stroke, transient ischemic attack, or seizure within 6 months prior to signing ICF.

12. Participant is pregnant, breastfeeding, or planning to become pregnant while enrolled in this study or within 6 months after the last dose of study treatment.

13. Presence of the following cardiac conditions:

- a. New York Heart Association stage III or IV congestive heart failure
- b. Myocardial infarction or coronary artery bypass graft ≤ 6 months prior to randomisation

c. History of clinically significant ventricular arrhythmia or unexplained syncope, not  believed to be vasovagal in nature or due to dehydration

d. Uncontrolled cardiac arrhythmia or clinically significant ECG abnormalities

14. Known to be seropositive for human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) or active HBV or HCV infection.

15. Chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) with a FEV1 &lt;50% of predicted. (FEV1 testing is required for subjects suspected of having COPD), Moderate or severe persistent asthma within the past 2 years, uncontrolled asthma of any classification.

16.  Concurrent  medical  or  psychiatric  condition  or  disease  that  is  likely  to  interfere  with  study procedures  or  results,  or  that  in  the  opinion  of  the  investigator  would  constitute  a  hazard  for participating in this study.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

17. Major surgery within 2 weeks prior to the start of administration of study treatment, or will not have fully recovered from surgery, or has major surgery planned during the time the participant is expected to be treated in the study or within 2 weeks after administration of the last dose of study treatment.

## Treatments

Prior to implementation of Amendment 6, this Phase 3 study had 2 parts. Part 1 is a randomised study comparing Tec-Dara versus investigator's choice of DPd/DVd in participants with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma. Following implementation of Amendment 6, Part 2 (a non-randomised, single-arm evaluation of a fixed-dose Tec-Dara regimen in the same population) was removed. No participants were enrolled in Part 2.

The study consisted of 3 phases: A Screening Phase, a Treatment Phase (Intervention Phase), and a  Follow-up  Phase  (Postintervention  Phase).  The  Screening  Phase  was  up  to  28  days  before randomisation.

In  the  Tec-Dara  group,  Teclistamab  and  daratumumab were administered sc in 28-day cycle as follows:

## Teclistamab:

- Cycle

1:

- Step-up dose 1 (0.06 mg/kg): Day 2
- Step-up dose 2 (0.3 mg/kg): Day 4
- Treatment Dose (1.5 mg/kg): Days 8, 15 and 22
- Cycle 2: Treatment Dose (1.5 mg/kg): Days 1, 8, 15 and 22
- Cycles 3-6: Treatment Dose (3 mg/kg): Days 1 and 15
- Cycle 7+: Treatment Dose (3 mg/kg):Day 1

Daratumumab (1800mg sc):

Cycles 1-2: Days 1, 8, 15 and 22

Cycles 3-6: Days 1 and 15

Cycle 7+: Day 1

In  the  DPd  group,  daratumumab, pomalidomide  and DXM were administered in 28-day cycle as follows:

## Daratumumab (1800mg sc):

Cycles 1-2: Days 1, 8, 15 and 22

Cycles 3-6: Days 1 and 15

Cycle 7+: Day 1

Pomalidomide (4mg po)

Day 1-21 on each cycle

DXM (40mg po, or 20mg po for participants &gt;75 years of age or with BMI &lt;18.5)

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Weekly (Days 1, 8, 15, and 22)

In the DVd group, daratumumab, pomalidomide and DXM were administered in 21-day cycle for cycles 1-8 and in 28-day cycle from Cycle 9 onwards, as follows:

## Daratumumab (1800mg sc):

Cycles 1-3: Days 1, 8, and 15

Cycles 4-8: Day 1

Cycle 9+: Day 1

Bortezomib (sc 1.3 mg/m 2 )

Cycles 1-8 only: Days 1, 4, 8 and 11

## DXM

Cycles 1-8 only: Days 1, 2, 4, 5, 8, 9, 11, and 12 (20 mg po/iv, for participants ≤ 75 years of age)

Cycles 1-8 only: Days 1 and 8 (20 mg po/iv, for participants &gt;75 years of age or BMI &lt;18.5)

## Pre-administration Medication

To decrease the risk of IRRs, all subjects received the following medications 1 to 3 hours prior to study drug administration:

- Dexamethasone  20  mg  or  equivalent  only  required  in  Cycle  1  on  days  1,  2,  4  and  8. Thereafter,  dexamethasone  was  required  only  if  the  participant  experienced  Grade  ≥2 CRS/sARR related to the previous dose of Teclistamab or daratumumab
- Paracetamol (acetaminophen) 650-1000 mg IV or orally (PO) prior to daratumumab injection in all cycles.
- An antihistamine: diphenhydramine 25-50 mg IV or PO, or equivalent prior to daratumumab injection in all cycles.
- Montelukast 10 mg 1-3 h prior to daratumumab injection (optional)

## Post-treatment  medication  for  daratumumab  sc  for  participants  with  higher  risk  of respiratory complications

For participants with a higher risk of respiratory complications (e.g., participants with mild asthma or participants with COPD who have an FEV1 &lt;80% at screening or developed FEV1 &lt;80% during the study without any medical history) the following post-infusion medications should be considered:

- Antihistamine (diphenhydramine or equivalent)
- Leukotriene inhibitor (montelukast or equivalent)
- Short-acting β 2 adrenergic receptor agonist such as salbutamol aerosol
- Control  medications  for  lung  disease  (e.g.,  inhaled  corticosteroids  ±  long-acting β 2 adrenergic receptor agonists for participants with asthma; long-acting bronchodilators such as tiotropiumor salmeterol ± inhaled corticosteroids for participants with COPD).

## Objectives

Primary objective

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The primary objective is to determine whether Tec-Dara will significantly improve PFS compared with investigator's choice of DPd/DVd in participants who have received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy, including a PI and lenalidomide.

## Secondary objective (selection)

The secondary objectives are:

To determine whether Tec-Dara will improve clinical outcome as measured by:

- Overall response rate (ORR)
- Very good partial response (VGPR) or better
- Complete response (CR) or better
- MRD negativity
- Progression-free survival on next-line therapy (PFS2)
- Time to next treatment (TTNT)
- Duration of response (DOR)
- OS (Overall survival)

Disease evaluations were performed by the central laboratory (M-protein and serum free light chain measurements,  and  immunofixation  determinations  in  serum  and  24-hour  urine)  during  the Treatment  Phase  for  all  participants  every  28  days  (±7  days),  except  in  Cycles  1-8  for  DVd participants  when  disease  evaluations  were  performed  every  21  days  (±7  days).  Response assessments were based on IMWG criteria (2016).

MRD  status  was  assessed  by  next  generation  sequencing  (NGS)  (clonoSEQ  test,  Adaptive Biotechnologies),  except  for  participants  enrolled  in  China  for  whom  only  next  generation  flow cytometry (NGF) (Euroflow test, Cytognos) was available at the time of study initiation; the primary analysis of MRD negativity was based on the MRD NGS Primary Analysis Population.

Response to treatment were determined by the investigator, through use of a validated algorithm, and through use of an Independent Review Committee (IRC).

## Endpoints

## Primary Endpoint

PFS as assessed by the IRC, defined as the time from the date of randomisation to the date of initial documented progression to MM in accordance with the IMWG diagnostic criteria for MM or the date of death, whichever occurred first.

## Secondary endpoints

- Objective Response Rate (ORR), defined as the proportion of participants with a PR or better as defined by the IMWG response criteria
- VGPR or better, defined as the proportion of participants with a VGPR or better as defined by the IMWG response criteria.
- CR rate or better, defined as the proportion of participants with a CR (or better) as defined by the IMWG response criteria.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- MRD negativity, defined as proportion of participants who achieve MRD negativity at a threshold of 10 - 5  at any timepoint after the date of randomisation and before disease progression or start of subsequent antimyeloma therapy.
- PFS2, defined as the time interval between the date of randomisation and date of event, which is  defined  as  progressive  disease  as  assessed  by  investigator  on  the  first  subsequent  line  of antimyeloma therapy, or death from any cause, whichever occurs first.
- Time to next-line treatment (TTNT) for MM, defined as, the time from randomisation to the start of subsequent antimyeloma treatment.
- Duration of response, defined as the time from the date of onset of first response until date of disease progression or death among responders, whichever occurs first.
- Overall Survival (OS), defined as the time from the date of randomisation to the date of death.
- PROs: The MySIm-Q, PROMIS Short Form v2.0 - Physical Functioning 8c, EORTC-QLQ-C30, PROCTCAE, and EQ-5D-5L utility and visual analog scores, and PGI-S. Time to worsening and time to  improvement  for  the  PRO  scores  will  be  measured  as  the  interval  from  the  date  of randomisation to the start date of meaningful change. Worsening was defined by a meaningful threshold (MID) based on PGI-S anchor-based methods (MySIm-Q) or based on the literature EORTC QLQ-C30) without subsequent improvement.

PRO instruments were to be completed within 2 days prior to dosing (except PRO-CTCAE) before any clinical tests (other than blood draws), procedures, or other consultations that would influence his or her perceptions of their current health state. During the Follow-up Phase, PROs were collected every 16 weeks (±14 days) after the EOT Visit, unless participation in a subsequent clinical study prohibited collection. PRO instruments, except PRO-CTCAE, continued to be administered even if subsequent antimyeloma therapy had begun.

## Sample size

Approximately 560 participants were to be randomised in a 1:1 ratio to achieve a total of 335 PFS events to provide approximately 90% power, with a two - sided alpha of 0.05, for the primary study objective of the study. This assumed 1) a median PFS of 18.6 months for Arm A and 13 months for Arm B, which translated into a HR of 0.70 (a median PFS improvement of 5.6 months), and 2) an accrual period of 18 months, an additional follow - up of 13 months, and an annual dropout rate of 5%. The sample size also accounted for one interim analysis for PFS.

Long - term survival (i.e., OS) follow - up is set to continue until approximately 335 deaths have been observed. Therefore, the study was to have approximately 80% power to detect a 27% reduction in the risk of death (HR = 0.73, assuming a median OS of 42.5 months for Arm A and 31 months for Arm B using a log - rank test with a two - sided alpha of 0.05.

## Randomisation

Central randomisation was implemented in this study. Participants were randomly assigned to 1 of 2 treatment arms in 1:1 ratio based on a computer-generated randomisation schedule and stratified by investigator ' s choice of DPd or DVd, stage of disease at screening per ISS (I vs II vs III), number of prior lines of therapy (1 vs 2 or 3), and prior anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody exposure.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Blinding (masking)

This is an open-label study. The IRC reviewed blinded efficacy data for the primary analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint.

## Statistical methods

The study estimand and the statistical methods were predefined in the study protocol and refined in a statistical analysis plan (SAP). The original SAP was dated 13 Jan 2022 and was first amended on 27 Jan 2023. The analyses presented here are based on the 2 nd  amendment that was finalised 02 Apr 2025, before the interim analysis CSR, for which the date of last observation recorded was 01 Aug 2025 and date of database lock 10 Sep 2025.

## Analysis of primary endpoint

The primary estimand was defined by the following 5 components:

- Population: participants who have received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy, including a PI and lenalidomide. All participants who previously received 1 line of therapy must be refractory to lenalidomide.
- Variable: PFS.
- Study intervention: combination of teclistamab and daratumumab SC (Tec-Dara [ArmA]) as compared to standard therapy (DPd/DVd).
- Intercurrent event:
- o study  intervention  switching  to  other  anticancer  therapy.  Hypothetical  strategy: censor  PFS  event  occurred  after  subsequent  therapy,  as  if  the  participants  had continued  treatment  as  planned  and  had  not  switched  to  subsequent  anticancer therapies.
- Population-level summary: hazard ratio of Tec-Dara (Arm A) vs DPd/DVd.

The primary analysis consisted of a 2-sided stratified log-rank test, stratified by investigator's choice of DPd or DVd, stage of disease at screening per ISS (I vs II vs III), number of prior lines of therapy (1 vs 2 or 3), and prior anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody exposure for the comparison of the PFS distribution between the treatment arms in the ITT population. Within each stratification factor, if 1 of the strata would have a very small sample size (i.e., less than 10% of total sample size), this stratum  was  to  be  considered  for  pooling  with  other  strata  in  the  stratified  analysis.  Hence, eventually, the log-rank test was stratified by ISS staging (I vs. II or III) and number of prior lines (1  vs.  2  or  3)  only.  The  treatment  effect  (HR)  and  its  two-sided  95%  confidence  intervals  were estimated using a stratified (as above) Cox regression model with treatment as the sole explanatory variable. The Kaplan-Meier method was used to estimate the distribution of overall PFS for each arm.

## Sensitivity analyses

The following sensitivity and supplementary analyses were conducted:

- using unstratified log-rank test and unstratified Cox's regression model
- with no censoring after missing two or more consecutive disease evaluation
- with no censoring due to start of subsequent anti-myeloma therapies for participants
- censoring  for  death  or  progression  after  treatment  regimen  discontinuation  (i.e.,  all treatment components) for toxicity or other reason

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- defining PD based on investigator assessment according to the IMWG response criteria
- defining PD based on computerized algorithm according to the IMWG response criteria

The kappa statistics  and  95% CI will  be  calculated  for  agreement  between  IRC  assessment  and computerized algorithm assessment for progression.

The RMST, measured by the area under the Kaplan-Meier PFS curve up to the selected specific time point was calculated for each treatment arm, where the selected timepoint is the smaller value of the longest PFS event time observed from either Arm A or Arm B. The difference in RMST and 95% CI were reported.

The restricted mean time lost (RMTL), defined as the area above the Kaplan-Meier curve up to the selected time point, and the ratio of RMTLs were also provided.

## Secondary endpoints

The analysis of binary endpoints (e.g., overall response, VGPR or better, CR/sCR) was conducted using  stratified  Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel  (CMH)  test.  For  MRD  analyses,  Fisher's  exact  test  was performed.  The  stratified  Cochran  Mantel-Haenszel  odds  ratio  was  provided  as  the  measure  of treatment effect along with its two-sided 95% CI. For time-to-event endpoints (e.g., PFS2, OS, and time of worsening of symptoms, and time to next treatment), similar statistical methods were applied as for PFS. For OS, data were analysed as per treatment policy strategy; time to death was used irrespective of switching to subsequent anti-myeloma therapy or not. For time to sustained worsening of  PROs,  death  due  to  disease  progression  will  be  considered  as  worsening.  Participants  without worsening of PRO were censored at the last assessment date of the MySIm-Q before the start of subsequent therapies.

For the quantitative PROs, LSMEANS were derived based on the mixed effects model with repeated measures,  in  which  the  dependent  variable  is  change  from  baseline  in  score,  and  independent variables are baseline, visit, treatment, visit by treatment interaction, and  randomisation stratification  factors  -  ISS  staging  (I  vs.  II  or  III)  and  number  of  prior  lines  (1  vs.  2  or  3),  as randomised. Unstructured covariance matrix was assumed.

## Subgroups

At the interim analysis, consistency and homogeneity of treatment effects on PFS and CR or better were evaluated across prespecified subgroups sex, age, race, and baseline status with respect to renal  function,  hepatic  function,  ECOG  performance  score,  ISS  stage,  presence  of  soft  tissue plasmacytomas and percentage of bone marrow plasma cells. Subgroup analyses were also done by investigators choice of DPd/DVd, number of lines of prior therapy, and genetic risk group.

## Control for multiplicity

Hierarchical test on the key secondary endpoints, including the endpoints to be formally tested and the order of the testing, were described in detail in the SAP.

If the primary endpoint PFS was statistically significant, then the following key secondary endpoints ordered below were to be tested sequentially for superiority, each at 0.05 (2-sided) significance level, by using a hierarchical procedure to control familywise Type I error rate at a 2-sided significance level of 0.05 (overall):

1. Response of CR or better
2. Overall response (PR or better)
3. MRD negativity

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

4. Overall survival
5. Time to worsening of symptoms in the MySIm-Q total symptom score

Initially, an interim analysis was set at approximately 65% of the total planned PFS events. Following health  authority  feedback,  the  timing  of  the  interim  analysis  was  updated  to  be  aligned  with approximately 75% (251) of the total planned 335 PFS events in Protocol Amendment 5. Additionally, this change resulted in an increase in the anticipated fraction of OS events from 33% to 40% at the time of the interim analysis. The significance level at this interim analysis to establish the superiority of  Tec-Dara  (Arm  A)  over  DPd/DVd  (Arm  B)  with  regard  to  PFS  was  determined  based  on  the observed number of PFS events at the interim analysis, using the O'Brien-Fleming boundaries as implemented by the Lan-DeMets alpha spending method. With 251 PFS events observed, the alpha to be spent at this interim analysis was to be 0.0193 (two-sided) but was to be adjusted based on the actual information fraction. If the observed two-sided p-value is smaller than this significance level, the superiority of Arm A (Tec-Dara) versus Arm B (DPd/DVd) with respect to PFS would be established. The final analysis for PFS was to be performed after 335 PFS events are reported.

For all key secondary endpoints listed above, except for OS, no formal group sequential testing would be performed. For OS, the first and second interim analyses would be performed at the same time as the interim and final analysis for PFS, respectively. A nominal alpha of 0.0001 (2-sided) would be spent for the first interim analysis. For the interim 2, 3, and final analysis, the alpha spending function would be a power spending function with parameter=2.

Of note, among treated participants in the comparator group (DPd/DVd), 263 participants (90.7%) received DPd and 27 participants (9.3%) received DVd.

## Recruitment

Study Initiation Date: 14 October 2021 (date first participant was screened).

Interim Completion Date: 01 August 2025 (Date of last observation recorded as part of the database for interim analysis). The analyses presented in this report are based on a database lock performed on 10 September 2025.

## Conduct of the study

The protocol was amended 9 times as summarised below.

The protocol amendments included the following notable changes:

- Global Amendment 2 (29 September 2021) modified teclistamab dosing from 1.5 mg/kg weekly to 3 mg/kg Q2W starting from Cycle 3 Day 1.
- Global Amendment 3 (25 May 2022) modified teclistamab dosing from 3 mg/kg Q2W to 3 mg/kg Q4W starting from Cycle 7 Day 1 and removed the requirement that no more than 60% of participants in Arm B would receive either DPd or DVd.
- Global Amendment 4 (22 September 2022) introduced a single-arm, fixed-dose Tec-Dara cohort (Part 2) to evaluate the PK, safety, and efficacy of a fixed-dose regimen. Additionally, a new section was added to outline management guidelines for hypogammaglobulinemia.
- Global Amendment 5 (30 January 2023) updated the timing of the interim analysis to approximately 75% of the total planned PFS events.
- Global  Amendment  6  (15  February  2023)  updated  the  protocol  after  fatal  infections  were observed in participants treated with fixed dose teclistamab in combination with daratumumab in the Phase 1 study TriMM-2. No participants in MajesTEC-3 had enrolled in the fixed-dose TecDara  cohort  (Part  2)  and  this  cohort  was  removed  in  Amendment  6.  This  amendment  also enhanced the guidance regarding immunoglobulin replacement.

An  ad  hoc  IDMC  was  convened  on  27  February  2023  to  review  unblinded  safety  data  from MajesTEC-3. The IDMC recommended that the study continue without modifications but noted that there was an imbalance in fatal infections in the Tec-Dara group compared with the control group although overall death rates were similar between the treatment groups. The IDMC also noted that the participants who experienced fatal infections in MajesTEC-3 had not been receiving immunoglobulin replacement as prophylaxis prior to the infection, which had also been observed in the fatal infection cases in TriMM-2. The IDMC recommended that the Sponsor take steps to address  access  barriers  to  immunoglobulin  replacement  and  improve  adherence  to  the prophylaxis  guidelines  outlined  in  the  protocol.  In  response,  the  following  actions  were undertaken by the Sponsor:

- o A letter was sent to all MajesTEC-3 investigators on 10 March 2023 to communicate the IDMC's findings and recommendations, including confirmation of reimbursement for IVIg.
- o Investigator meetings were held to review the TriMM-2 data, discuss the IDMC's findings, and  emphasize  the  importance  of  immunoglobulin  replacement  therapy,  infection prophylaxis, and infection management.
- o The Sponsor secured a central supply of IVIg for study participants.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- o Medical monitoring of hypogammaglobulinemia was intensified across all participants, including subject-level reviews to identify those not receiving IVIg. Direct communication with  sites  was  initiated  to  understand  barriers  and  reinforce  adherence  to  the immunoglobulin replacement guidelines newly specified in the protocol.

The IDMC continued monitoring safety in MajesTEC-3 every 3 months until November 2023, after which  monitoring  resumed  every  6  months  as  per  the  protocol.  At  each  meeting,  the  IDMC recommended the study proceed without modifications. On 20 November 2023, the committee also  commented  that  there  had  been  a  decrease  in  fatal  infections  since  implementation  of Amendment 6 in February 2023 and IVIg administration had increased. The IDMC encouraged the continued use of IVIg prophylaxis to further enhance patient safety.

- Global Amendment 7 (11 May 2023) consolidated existing infection prophylaxis and management content to facilitate compliance by investigative sites (e.g., for guidance related to hepatitis B reactivation  and  COVID-19  infection),  as  well  as  added  new  content  regarding  infection prophylaxis and management. Additionally, it was clarified that following any dosing interruption of teclistamab or daratumumab, there must be no active bacterial, viral, or fungal infection before proceeding to the next dose of either study drug. This amendment also added additional details on prophylactic immunoglobulin replacement (IVIg) use. Specifically, guidance in the protocol recommended prophylactic immunoglobin replacement to maintain serum IgG levels ≥ 400 mg/dL due to the susceptibility of patients with multiple myeloma to infections.
- Global Amendment 8 (18 July 2024) modified guidance for repeat step - up dosing after prolonged teclistamab dose interruption and further modified guidance for prophylactic IVIg replacement.
- Global Amendment 9 (27 January 2025) removed sample collections for biomarker and MRD assessments that were no longer required, as sufficient samples had been collected for analyses.

## Protocol deviations

All protocol deviations of eligibility criteria and those deviations that could impact participant safety or the primary endpoint were considered major protocol deviations. Major protocol deviations were limited and balanced between the 2 treatment groups ( Table 10 ). Protocol deviations did not have a significant impact on the overall results of the study.

## Outcomes and estimation

## Primary endpoint: PFS by IRC

With a median follow-up of 34.5 months in the ITT population, a total of 231 PFS events (Tec-Dara: 44; control: 187) were observed. The HR for the Tec-Dara group vs control group was 0.17 (95% CI: 0.12, 0.23), demonstrating a reduction in the risk of disease progression or death for participants randomised to Tec-Dara (p &lt;0.0001, crossing the O'Brien-Fleming stopping boundary of 0.0139 in favour of the Tec-Dara group).

Median PFS was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 18.1 months (95% CI: 14.6, 22.8) in the control group ( Table 16 and Figure 11 ).

Figure 11. Kaplan-Meier Plot for PFS Based on IRC Assessment; Intent-to-treat Analysis Set, MajesTEC-3

<!-- image -->

The most common reason for censoring was administrative: 221 and 83 were censored due to study cut-off in Tec-Dara and DPd/DVd, respectively. Of the remaining participants censored, 12 in TecDara and 17 in DPd/DVd were censored due to start of subsequent antimyeloma therapy prior to disease progression. The number of those who were censored despite of observed progression or death because of missing two or more consecutive missed visits were 3 and 2, respectively.

## Subgroup Analysis

Subgroup analyses of PFS was consistent across all pre-specified and clinically relevant subgroups ( Figure 12 ).

All sensitivity and supplementary analyses showed similar results to the primary PFS analysis (data not shown).

## Key secondary efficacy endpoints

## CR or better

The rate of CR or better based on IRC assessment was statistically significantly higher in the TecDara group than the control group (81.8% vs 32.1%; OR=9.56 [95% CI: 6.47, 14.14]; p&lt;0.0001) ( Table 17 ).

## Overall response

The rate of overall response (PR or better) based on IRC assessment was statistically significantly higher in the Tec-Dara group than the control group (89.0% vs 75.3%; OR=2.65 [95% CI: 1.68, 4.18]; p&lt;0.0001.

In the MRD NGS Primary Analysis Population, the MRD-negative CR rate (at 10 -6 ) in the Tec-Dara group was 53.8% vs 10.4% in the control group; OR=9.70 [95% CI: 6.11, 15.42]; p&lt;0.0001.

## Overall Survival

As of the clinical cutoff, 46 participants had died in the Tec-Dara group vs 98 participants in the control group, and the HR for the Tec-Dara group vs control group was 0.46 (95% CI: 0.32, 0.65) (p&lt; 0.0001) ( Table 19 , Figure 13 ).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

It was observed that the two Kaplan-Meier curves crossed at around 10 months after randomisation, indicating an observed higher risk of death in the Tec-Dara group early on primarily due to fatal infections  which  occurred  in  the  early  stage  of  the  study.  To  obtain  further  insight  into  the  OS treatment effect over time in the presence of non-proportional hazards pattern, additional analyses were performed as described below.

Restricted Mean Survival Time (RMST) and Restricted Mean Time Loss (RMTL)

- RMST difference (Tec-Dara minus control): 2.15 months (95% CI: 0.54, 3.77); p-value=0.0088
- Ratio of RMTL (Tec-Dara to control): 0.64 (95% CI: 0.45, 0.91); p-value=0.0124

## Piecewise Analysis

In the 0 - ≤ 10 months period, there were equal numbers of OS events between the treatment groups with HR=1.08 (95% CI: 0.64, 1.81). In the &gt;10 months period, the HR was 0.22 (95% CI: 0.13, 0.38) with substantially fewer OS events reported in the Tec-Dara group than the control group.

Subgroup analyses of OS in pre-specified subgroups is presented in Figure 14 .

## Other efficacy endpoints

## Time to Worsening of Symptoms in the MySIm -Q Total Symptom Score

According  to  MAH,  the  MySIm-Q  is  a  disease-specific  PRO  assessment  with  established  content validity  for  patients  with  relapsed  or  refractory  multiple  myeloma  and  newly  diagnosed  multiple myeloma.

The median time to worsening of multiple myeloma symptoms based on the MySIm-Q total symptom subscale was not reached for the Tec-Dara group and was 39.9 months (95% CI: 36.3, NE) for the control. The HR (Tec-Dara vs control) was 0.50 (95% CI: 0.34, 0.72) (p=0.0002), which indicated a statistically significant 50% risk reduction in worsening of symptoms for the Tec-Dara group ( Table 20 ).
