The most common reasons for censoring were 'study cutoff' (Tec-Dara: 72% of participants; control: 29.6% of participants) and 'start of subsequent antimyeloma therapy' (Tec-Dara: 9.6%; control: 49.1%)

## Stringent CR

The rate of sCR based on IRC assessment was higher in the Tec-Dara group (77.3% [95% CI: 72.1%, 82.0%]) than the control group (23.3% [95% CI: 18.6%, 28.6%]).

## VGPR or better

The rate of VGPR or better based on IRC assessment was higher in the Tec-Dara group than the control group (86.6% vs 57.1%; OR=4.85 [95% CI: 3.21, 7.32]).

## PFS 2

Fewer  PFS  2  (PFS  on  next  line  of  therapy)  events  were  observed  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  (47 participants, 16.2%) vs control (142 participants, 48.0%): HR=0.29 (95% CI: 0.21, 0.40).

## Time to Next Treatment

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The median time to subsequent antimyeloma therapy was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 24.4 months (95% CI: 19.5, 28.0) in the control group (HR=0.14 [95% CI: 0.10, 0.20].

## Duration of Response

The median DOR in responders (with a PR or better) based on IRC assessment was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 23.5 months (95% CI: 19.8, 29.9) in the control group. The probability of responders remaining in response at 24 and 36 months was higher in the Tec-Dara group than the control group: 24.month event-free rate of 90.1% in Tec-Dara vs 49.2% in control, and.36month event-free rate of 88.5% in Tec-Dara vs 36.4% in control.

## Time to Response

The median time to first response (PR or better based on IRC assessment) was rapid, occurring by 1.2 months of treatment in the Tec-Dara group; the median time to CR or better was 6.9 months.

## Time to Disease Progression

The median time to disease progression based on IRC assessment was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 20.7 months (95% CI: 16.1, 24.2) in the control group (HR=0.10 [95% CI: 0.06, 0.15].

## Patient-Reported Outcomes

The compliance rate for PRO assessments was high and comparable between treatment groups. It was &gt;87% at baseline and &gt;80% at most subsequent timepoints, ranging from 78.3% to 100% for the MySIm-Q.

## MySIm -Q

In  general,  higher  percentages  of  participants  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  achieved  meaningful improvements in the MySIm-Q total symptom score and total impact score than in the control group.

## EQ-5D-5L

At Cycle 37 Day 1, the LS Means (95% CI) of change from baseline visual analogue scale score was 5.273 (3.031, 7.514) for the Tec-Dara group and 2.893 (-0.103, 5.889) for the control group. At Cycle 37 Day 1, the LS Means (95% CI) of change from baseline of utility score was 0.057 (0.027, 0.087) for the Tec-Dara group and 0.047 (0.004, 0.09) for the control group.

## PROMIS -PF

At Cycle 37 Day 1, the LS Means of change from baseline (95% CI) was 2.385 (1.104, 3.665) for Tec-Dara and 0.557 (-1.208, 2.322) for control; 2-sided nominal p=0.0964.

## EORTC -QLQC30

The LS Means of change from baseline scores showed improvement in Global Health Status through Cycle 37 (5.433 [95% CI: 2.567, 8.299] for Tec-Dara and 0.26 [95% CI: -3.716, 4.237] for control; 2-sided nominal p=0.0367.

- Functional and Symptom Subscales

Adjusted mean change from baseline results from the EORTC QLQ-C30 scale scores were consistent, demonstrating between-group differences in favor of Tec-Dara.

## Exploratory efficacy endpoints

## Sustained MRDnegative CR or Better Rate

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In the MRD NGS Primary Analysis Population, the rate of sustained ( ≥ 6 months) MRD-negative CR at an MRD sensitivity threshold of 10 -5  in the Tec-Dara group was 46.6% for Tec-Dara vs 11.2% for control group (OR=6.78 [95% CI: 4.31, 10.64].

The rate of sustained ( ≥ 6 months) MRD-negative CR at a sensitivity threshold of 10 -6  in the Tec- Dara group was 39.3% for Tec-Dara vs 6.3% for control group (OR=9.20 [95% CI: 5.31, 15.95].

## MRDnegative CR Rate at Pre -specified Timepoints

- MRD-negative CR (at 10 -5 ) at 6 months: 35.1% for Tec-Dara vs 7.8% for control (OR=6.22 [95% CI: 3.73, 10.37]).
- MRD-negative CR (at 10 -5 ) at 12 months: 50.0% for Tec-Dara vs 14.1% for control (OR=6.08 [95% CI: 3.99, 9.26].

## MRDnegativity Rate by Cytogenetic Risk Group at Baseline

- MRD-negativity rates (at 10 -5 ) in the high-risk cytogenetics group were 58.9% for Tec-Dara vs 9.7% for control (OR=12.57 [95% CI: 5.61, 28.18]).
- MRD-negativity rates (at 10 -5 ) in the standard-risk cytogenetics group were 63.6% for TecDara vs 24.8% for control (OR=5.23 [95% CI: 2.98, 9.20]).

## MRD Negativity and Long -term Outcomes

In participants with MRD-negative CR, PFS events were reported for only 6.6% of participants in the Tec-Dara group vs 34.8% in the control group; median PFS was not reached in either treatment group (HR=0.16 [95% CI: 0.07, 0.35]).

In  participants  who  did  not  achieve  MRD-negative  CR,  PFS  events  were  reported  for  27.0%  of participants in the Tec-Dara group vs 68.6% in the control group,  median PFS was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 14.0 months in the control group (HR=0.32 [95% CI: 0.22, 0.48];). A Kaplan-Meier  plot  for  PFS  based  on  MRD-negative  CR  status  in  the  MRD  NGS  Primary  Analysis Population is provided in Figure 15 .

An analysis of PFS based on MRD-negative CR status at the 10 -6  threshold in the MRD NGS Primary Analysis Population also demonstrated a substantial improvement in PFS in the Tec-Dara group vs control group both in participants who achieved MRD-negative CR and those who did not (data not shown).

## Progression - free Survival by Cytogenetic Risk Group at Baseline

Analysis  of  PFS  based  on  cytogenetic  risk  at  baseline  in  the  ITT  Population  demonstrated  an improvement in PFS in the Tec-Dara group vs control group in participants with high- and standardrisk cytogenetics.

In participants with high-risk cytogenetics, PFS events were reported for 19.2% of participants in the Tec-Dara group vs 75.0% in the control group; median PFS was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 14.4 months in the control group.

The 12-, 24-, and 36- month PFS rates were 88.2%, 81.7%, and 77.7%, respectively, in the TecDara group and 54.9%, 25.4%, and 11.5%, respectively, in the control group.

In participants with standard-risk cytogenetics, PFS events were reported for 11.9% of participants in the Tec-Dara group vs 57.9% in the control group; median PFS was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 24.7 months in the control group. The 12-, 24-, and 36- month PFS rates were 89.9%,

87.4%, and 87.4%, respectively, in the Tec-Dara group and 64.5%, 52.6%, and 37.1%, respectively, in the control group.

## Analysis performed across trials (pooled analyses and meta-analysis)

Not applicable.

## Clinical studies in special populations

Not applicable.

## Supportive study

Trimm-2 (also referred to as 64407564MMY100) : A phase 1b study of subcutaneous daratumumab regimens in combination with bispecific T-cell redirection antibodies for the treatment of subjects with multiple myeloma

This  is  an  open-label,  multicenter,  multi-cohort  study  of  daratumumab  in  combination  with teclistamab  or  another  bispecific  antibody  (talquetamab),  with  and  without  pomalidomide,  in participants with multiple myeloma who have received at least 3 prior lines of therapy (including a PI and an IMiD) or who have disease that is double refractory to a PI and an IMiD.

The study was conducted in 2 parts:

Part 1: Dose Escalation to establish the RP2Ds of each treatment combination

Part 2: Dose Expansion at the RP2Ds for selected treatment combinations.

The aim of Part 1 was to evaluate the safety of teclistamab in combination with daratumumab and to evaluate preliminary antitumor activity.

Part 2 was intended to evaluate the safety, PK, pharmacodynamics, and preliminary clinical activity of the treatment combination at the RP2D identified in Part 1 of the study.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Only data from teclistamab`s RP2D-cohort is considered relevant (doses similar to the dose schedule of teclistamab in MajesTEC-3) and presented briefly below.

## Study Disposition

Sixty-one participants in the RP2D cohorts (i.e., treated with 1.5 mg/kg weekly or 3.0 mg/kg Q2W teclistamab) received a dose of any study treatment on or before the CCO of 31 January 2024 and were included in the Safety Analysis Set for this population.

At the time of the CCO, 44 participants (72.1%) in the RP2D cohorts had discontinued the study. Nineteen  participants  (31.1%)  discontinued  the  study  after  starting  subsequent  therapy;  12 participants (19.7%) had died; 9 participants (14.8%) discontinued after completing the per-protocol 16-week follow-up period, 3 participants (4.9%) withdrew consent, and 1 participant (1.6%) was lost to follow-up. As of the CCO, 17 participants (27.9%) remained on study. The median duration of follow-up was 12.0 months.

## Treatment Disposition

As of the CCO, 45 participants (73.8%) in the RP2D cohorts had discontinued treatment with both study  drugs.  The  primary  reasons  for  discontinuation  of  the  last-stopped  study  treatment  were disease  progression  (16  participants,  35.6%)  and  TEAEs  (10  participants,  22.2%),  followed  by physician decision and ' Other ' (7 participants each, 15.6%).

## Extent of Exposure

Participants in the RP2D cohorts received a median of 9.2 months of therapy (range: 0.1 to 41.2) with teclistamab and a median of 8.1 months of therapy (range: 0.0 to 41.2) with daratumumab. Overall, 41 participants (67.2%) were treated with teclistamab for at least 6cycles, 25 (41.0%) were treated for at least 12 cycles, 17 (27.9%) were treated for at least 18 cycles, and 9 (14.8%) were treated for at least 24 cycles.

Forty participants (65.6%) were treated with daratumumab for at least 6 cycles, 22 (36.1%) were treated for at least 12 cycles, and 10 (16.4%) were treated for at least 18 cycles. The discontinuation of  daratumumab  in  participants  who  were  refractory  to  prior  anti-CD38  therapy  following  the implementation of the USM contributed to fewer participants receiving daratumumab beyond Cycle 12. Excluding participants who received repeat step-up doses, the median time from Step-up Dose 1 to the first treatment dose of teclistamab was 6.1 days (range: 4.1 to 27.0).

The median relative dose intensity, defined as total cumulative dose administered divided by the total cumulative dose planned, was 83.9% (range: 22.1% to 107.7%) for teclistamab and 95.2% (range: 40.0% to 100.0%) for daratumumab.

Thirteen participants who were refractory to prior anti-CD38 therapy in the RP2D cohorts were still receiving  daratumumab  at  the  time  of  the  implementation  of  the  USM.  All  13  discontinued daratumumab subsequent to the USM and Amendment 7. The median duration of follow-up before discontinuation  of  daratumumab  was  longer  than  after  discontinuation  of  daratumumab  (15.01 months and 9.9 months, respectively).

Demographic and Other Baseline Characteristics: The median age of participants in the RP2D cohorts (N=61) was 66.0 years (range: 40.0 to 81.0), the median time from diagnosis of multiple myeloma to enrolment in the study was 6.5 years (range: 0.7 to 20.9), and the median number of lines of prior therapy was 5.0 (1.0 to 14.).

Overall, 78.7% of participants received more than 3 lines of prior multiple myeloma therapy, 77.0% were triple-class exposed (PI, IMiD, and anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody), and 59.0% were penta-

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

exposed (at least 2 PIs, at least 2 IMiDs, and anti-CD38 monoclonal antibodies). Additionally, 90.2% of  participants  were  refractory  to  their  last  line  of  therapy,  73.8%  were  refractory  to  anti-CD38 monoclonal  antibodies,  63.9%  were  triple-class  refractory  (PI,  IMiD,  and  anti-CD38  monoclonal antibody), and 32.8% were penta-refractory.

Teclistamab administered at the selected RP2Ds in combination with daratumumab had promising efficacy in participants with heavily pretreated relapsed/refractory multiple myeloma, including an ORR of 68.9%, a VGPR or better rate of 63.9%, and a CR or better rate of 44.3%.

Responses were durable, with the probability of responders remaining in response at 12 and 24 months at 73.8% at 66.9%, respectively. The median PFS (26.3 months) was not yet mature. The 12- and 24-month PFS rates were 58.3% and 51.0%, respectively. Although limited by the small sample size, there was a trend of a slightly improved ORR (75.0% and 66.7%, respectively), and a trend of a higher probability of responders remaining in response at 24 months (82.5% and 60.4%, respectively) reported in the non-refractory subgroup when compared with the refractory subgroup.

However, regardless of refractory status, the depth of response and the DOR were improved when compared with teclistamab monotherapy.

## 2.4.2. Discussion on clinical efficacy

## Design and conduct of clinical studies

With this extension of indication application, the MAH is seeking approval for the following indication of teclistamab: in combination with daratumumab for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least one prior therapy.

This  application  is  based  upon  the  interim  PFS  results  of  the  pivotal  phase  3  study  MajesTEC-3 (64007957MMY3001), a randomised (1:1), open-label, multi-centre, phase 3 study that evaluated the  efficacy  and  safety  teclistamab  in  combination  with  subcutaneous  daratumumab  (Tec-Dara) versus  subcutaneous  daratumumab,  pomalidomide,  and  dexamethasone  (DPd)  or  subcutaneous daratumumab,  bortezomib,  and  dexamethasone  (DVd)  in  subjects  with  relapsed  or  refractory multiple myeloma. Randomisation was stratified by investigator's choice of DPd or DVd, stage of disease at screening per ISS (I vs II vs III), number of prior lines of therapy (1 vs 2 or 3), and prior anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody exposure. A total of 587 participants were randomised to Tec - Dara (N=291) or DPd/DVd (N=296).

The  stratification  factors  have  prognostic  implications  in  this  setting  and  are  deemed  clinically appropriate, apart from being too many (see discussion on statistical methods).

## Study participants

Inclusion  and  exclusion  criteria  are  overall  acceptable.  Key  inclusion  criteria  were  relapsed  or refractory multiple myeloma  according to IMWG  criteria with measurable disease. Since refractoriness  to  an  anti-CD38  monoclonal  antibody  was  an  exclusion  criterion,  the  number  of participants with previous exposure to an anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody was low and thus not in line with the current treatment paradigm, in which daratumumab or isatuximab are used in first line treatment. Participants with any prior BCMA-directed therapy were also excluded from the study.

Participants with CNS/meningeal involvement by MM or other severe neurological conditions were excluded from trial participation. Patients with low cardiac ejection fraction or patients with rarer forms  of  plasma  cell  neoplasms,  such  as  plasma  cell  leukaemia,  primary  systemic  light-chain

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

amyloidosis,  Waldenstrom  macroglobulinemia,  or  POEMS  syndrome  were  also  excluded.  As requested, this information has been added to section 5.1 of the SmPC.

Since there was a requirement of lenalidomide refractoriness in participants who have received only 1 prior line of antimyeloma therapy which is in line with the current clinical practice and the approved DPd indication, the combination of two different active substances (anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody + an immudomodulator) + DXM is considered an appropriate comparator.

The acceptability of the other comparator, DVd, is a more complicated issue. Bortezomib is clearly positioned as part of the first line treatment in different combinations, and as such reintroducing it later (if tolerated in the first line) due to cumulative toxicity (i.e. neuropathy) might not be possible in a considerable number of patients. It is possible that due to the above-mentioned reason only a low number of patients were chosen to be treated with the DVd regimen (27 participants). Therefore, the study is actually a comparison of Tec-Dara to DPd, and concerns related to potential re-exposure do not have an impact on overall results.

## Dose and administration regimen

Teclistamab  and  daratumumab  were  administered  sc  in  a  28-day  cycle.  Daratumumab  was administered according to the established product information of the product. Teclistamab included a step-up dosing on days 2, 4 and 8 (slightly modified compared to monotherapy), followed by a maintenance schedule of 1. 5 mg /kg weekly through Week 8, then 3 mg/kg once every two weeks through Week 24, and then 3 mg/kg once every four weeks thereafter until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.

Comparator treatment regimens are standard, and treatments were administered according to the SmPC of daratumumab (Darzalex 1800 mg solution for injection, s.c.).

## Objectives/endpoints

The primary objective was to determine whether Tec-Dara will significantly improve PFS compared with investigator's choice of DPd/DVd in participants who have received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy, including a PI and lenalidomide.

The primary endpoint was PFS by IRC which is considered acceptable. The other endpoints include response rates (ORR, VGPR or better, CR or better), MRD negativity, PFS2, time to next treatment, duration of response and OS, among others. These endpoints are established ones in MM, and as such appropriate.

Although classified as one of the secondary endpoints included in the statistical hierarchy, the interpretability of the PRO data is hampered by the study design and strategy for PRO assessments and analysis.

## Statistical methods

The multitude of stratification factors led to the need to pool strata together and eventually analyses were performed as if randomisation had been stratified by ISS and number of prior lines of therapy only. Otherwise, analysis of the primary endpoint, PFS based in IRC assessment, was conventional applying  stratified  log-rank  test  and  stratified  Cox  proportional  hazards  model.  The  fact  that participants were censored when starting subsequent antimyeloma treatment in absence of disease progression or due to missed visits - neither being supported by the relevant EMA guideline - is not considered an issue since there were relatively few such instances and the results of the sensitivity analyses without such censoring were consistent.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Regarding control of type 1 error, hierarchical testing order for the secondary endpoints was defined in the first version of SAP, dated 13 Jan 2022, i.e., just three months after screening the first patient. In this respect, the plan can be considered pre-defined. For the last endpoint in the hierarchy, time to worsening of symptoms in the MySIm-Q total symptom score, the threshold for worsening was data-driven and determined not long before the database lock.

The plan on interim analysis timing for PFS was changed to occur by 75% of the targeted event count (from initial 65%), and the actual timing corresponds to 69% information fraction. These changes and  deviations  from  the  plan  could,  in  principle,  be  a  concern  in  an  open-label  trial.  However, considering the observed, these concerns are only theoretical.

## Study conduct

Protocol amendments have been appropriately justified and are not believed to have affected trial integrity.

In Amendment 3, the requirement that no more than 60% of participants in Arm B would receive either DPd or DVd was removed which is acknowledged.

Amendment 4 introduced a management guideline for hypogammaglobulinemia. Nevertheless, and due to imbalance in fatal infections in the Tec-Dara group compared with the comparator group, the IDMC  encouraged  the  continued  use  of  IVIg  prophylaxis  to  further  enhance  patient  safety (Amendment 7). However, from the data provided it is not clear whether all fatal infections were related to low IgG levels, and as such whether the proposed recommendation to maintain the serum levels above &gt;400 mg/dl (modification to SmPC section 4.4) is based on the results observed from the ongoing study MajesTEC-3 or from other sources. The MAH reiterated that the proposed cut-off level of S-IgG &gt;400 mg/dl is based on recommendations of clinical guidelines and is aligned with the MajesTEC-3 protocol. The requirement for IVIg treatment was reinforced during the study and no association between the IgG levels and fatal infections could be observed. However, considering the high incidence of severe infections, and similar recommendations of S-IgG threshold level in the SmPC of two other T-cell engagers and one CAR-T cell product, the recommendation to maintain SIgG level ≥ 400 mg/dL during teclistamab treatment is acceptable.

Major protocol deviations were few and mostly balanced between study arms. Protocol deviations are not believed to have had a major impact on trial conduct or study results.

## Baseline characteristics

The demographic and clinical characteristics of the participants at baseline were generally balanced in the two groups and reflect a rather young and fit patient population with median age of 64 (range: 25 to 88) years. Half of the participants were less than 65 years, and thus not totally representing an average (older) MM patient in first or later relapse.

The proportion of participants with an ECOG performance score of 0, 1 and 2 was 55.7%, 40%, and 4.3%,  respectively,  and  balanced  between  groups.  Cytogenetic  risk  factors  were  also  overall balanced between study groups.

Most of the participants have received 2 or more lines of prior therapies, with a median of 2 lines in both groups. Importantly, approximately 40% of the participants had one previous line of treatment providing sufficient evidence for use of teclistamab in the proposed second-line indication.

## Efficacy data and additional analyses

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The study met its primary endpoint and demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in PFS in the Tec-Dara group compared with the DPd/DVd group. With a median follow-up of 34.5 months in the ITT population, median PFS was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 18.1 months (95% CI: 14.6, 22.8) in the comparator group [HR 0.17 (95% CI: 0.12, 0.23), p &lt;0.0001)]. Results of all sensitivity analyses were consistent with the primary PFS analysis.

Importantly, the study also showed an OS benefit of Tec-Dara with HR of 0.46 (95% CI: 0.32, 0.65); p&lt;0.0001. However, the KM curve for OS showed that participants in the Tec-Dara group have an increased risk of early death, compared to participants in the comparator group. This phenomenon is primarily due to fatal infections which occurred in the early stage of the study. This is of importance, since  especially  in  second  line  treatment  several,  perhaps  less  toxic  but  effective  alternative therapies, are available. However, as no distinct patient subgroup could be identified as being at increased risk of early death specific to Tec-Dara treatment the CHMP agreed that no specific warning in Section 4.4 of the SmPC was warranted.

All key secondary endpoints, including ORR, CR or better and MRD negativity rate, were in line with the primary endpoint favoring Tec-Dara group compared to DPd/DVd group.

The proposed indication wording for the treatment of patients who have received at least one prior treatment  is  considered  acceptable.  Although  all  patients  in  the  pivotal  trial  were  exposed  to lenalidomide  and  82.5%  were  lenalidomide  refractory,  it  is  not  considered  necessary  to  include requirement for prior lenalidomide treatment in the indication wording. Lenalidomide in different combinations  (followed  by  lenalidomide  maintenance)  will  be  used  until  disease  progression  or intolerance already in first line if considered the most suitable treatment option. The approved second line indications vary in terms of mentioning requirements for specific prior treatments. The rationale varies  depending  on  treatment  combinations  and  patient  populations  enrolled  in  the  trials.  For example, when treatment combination includes an agent that is only approved post a specific prior treatment, or two drugs from the same class are used in sequence, this information may be more crucial.  The  proposed  treatment  combination  does  not  include  an  IMID,  and  daratumumab  is approved in different combinations from 1L, and thus requirement for one prior line of therapy is considered sufficient.

In addition, although daratumumab treatment is increasingly given already in first line, and there is very limited data from anti-CD38 exposed patients, it is not considered necessary to limit treatment to non-anti-CD38 exposed patients. There are multiple approved treatment options for 2L myeloma patients, and potential re-exposure to daratumumab is best left for clinical consideration. Further, no limitations have been mentioned in various daratumumab indications.

## 2.4.3. Conclusions on the clinical efficacy

The addition of teclistamab to daratumumab translates into a significant delay in the progression of the disease in the targeted patient population, i.e. patients with multiple myeloma who have received at  least  one  prior  line  of  therapy.  This  improvement  is  considered  clinically  significant  and  is supported by key secondary endpoints including OS.

## 2.5. Clinical safety

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Introduction

The safety profile of the combination treatment of teclistamab and daratumumab (Tec-Dara) is based on the primary study MajesTEC-3. Unless otherwise noted, the CCO date of the MajesTEC-3 (01 Aug 2025) corresponds to the first protocol - specified interim analysis for the primary endpoint (PFS) that was to be performed after approximately 251 PFS events (approximately 75% of the total planned PFS events) were observed, to evaluate cumulative interim safety and efficacy data.

Supportive safety data is provided from the RP2D cohorts of the Study 64407564MMY1002 (referred to as TriMM-2).

## Patient exposure

The MajesTEC-3 Safety Analysis Set was comprised of 573 participants (Tec-Dara: n=283; control: n=290) who received any part of study treatment. In the safety population, 82/283 participants (29.0%) in the Tec-Dara group and 208/290 participants (71.7%) in the control group discontinued study treatment. The most common reason for discontinuation of study treatment in each treatment group was progressive disease.

## Duration of follow-up

As of the CCO, the median duration of follow-up in the ITT Population was 34.5 months (range: 0.03 to 45.3) and was similar between treatment groups (Tec-Dara: 34.7 months [range: 0.03 to 44.6]; control: 34.1 months [range: 0.16 to 45.3]).

## Exposure to treatment

The median duration of treatment was twice as long in the Tec-Dara group (32.4 months [range: 0.03 to 43.4]) as in the control group (16.1 months [range: 0.03 to 45.2]). At the time of the CCO, 81.6%  of  participants  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  reached  at  least  12  months  on  study  treatment compared with 58.3% in the control group; 73.1% of participants in the Tec-Dara group reached at least  24  months  on  study  treatment  compared  with  39.7%  in  the  control  group;  and  26.9%  of participants in the Tec-Dara group reached at least 36 months on study treatment compared with 9.3% in the control group.

The median relative dose intensity of teclistamab in the Tec-Dara group was 97.1%. The median relative dose intensity of teclistamab in the Tec-Dara group remained high across the treatment cycle intervals  (98.8%,  99.4%,  98%,  and  99.8%  for  Cycles  1,  2,  3-6,  and  7+,  respectively).  Median relative dose intensities of daratumumab were 97.8% in participants who received Tec-Dara, 95.5% in participants who received DPd, and 90.0% in participants who received DVd. The median relative dose intensity of daratumumab in the Tec-Dara group remained high across treatment cycle interval.

## Cycle delays, dose modifications, and dose reductions

Cycle delays were reported for 87.6% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and 64.1% of participants in the control group. An AE was the most frequently reported reason for cycle delays in both groups.

Dose modifications (dose delays, skips, or reductions) for teclistamab in the Tec-Dara group occurred in 81.9% of participants. Dose modifications of daratumumab, including delays and skips, occurred in 68.6% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and 76.9% in the control group. The median relative dose intensity of teclistamab and daratumumab in the Tec-Dara group remained high across the cycles.

Per protocol, dose reductions of teclistamab during the step-up dosing and first treatment dose were not permitted. Dose reductions of teclistamab for subsequent treatment dosing could be used to

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

manage toxicities. In the Tec-Dara group, 28.5% of participants had a teclistamab dose reduction (refers to reduction of protocol-defined cumulative teclistamab dose per cycle, including reducing the teclistamab dose from 3 mg/kg to 1.5 mg/kg or decreasing the dosing frequency). The majority of dose reductions occurred in cycle 7 and beyond. All teclistamab dose reductions were due to an AE.

Dose reductions of daratumumab were not permitted as per the protocol.

## Concomitant medications administered for specific situations

## Prophylactic antimicrobial medications

Prophylactic antivirals were prescribed for 246 participants (86.9%) in the Tec-Dara group and 235 participants (81.0%) in the control group, most commonly acyclovir (63.6% in the Tec-Dara group and 60.7% in the control group).

## Systemic steroid use

Per  the  protocol,  participants  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  were  not  to  receive  dexamethasone  as pretreatment medication for teclistamab after Cycle 1 Day 8, while participants in the control group received dexamethasone as part of their treatment regimen. Accordingly, the overall use of steroids during the study was significantly lower in the Tec-Dara group than in the control group, as indicated by the lower median total steroid dose received by participants in the Tec-Dara group (120.0 mg) vs the control group (1,329.8 mg).

## Immunoglobulin use

Overall,  a  higher  number  of  participants  received  IVIg  in  the  Tec-Dara  group:  247  participants (87.3%) in the Tec-Dara group and 130 participants (44.8%) in the control group received at least 1 dose of IVIg or SC immunoglobulin treatment as a concomitant medication. Additional guidance was added to the protocol (Global Protocol Amendment 6 dated 15 Feb 2023 and Global Protocol Amendment 7 dated 11 May 2023) to reinforce the recommended prophylactic IVIg replacement use to ensure participantsmaintained serum IgG levels ≥400 mg/dL due to the susceptibility of patients with multiple myeloma to infections.

## Growth factor use

Growth factors were utilized similarly in both treatment groups with 192 participants (67.8%) in the Tec-Dara group and 200 participants (69.0%) in the control group, with filgrastim being the most used in 137 participants (48.4%) and 144 participants (49.7%), respectively.

## Adverse events

## Overall summary of adverse events

Overall,  the  incidence  of  TEAEs,  Grade  3,  4  and  TEAEs  were  similar  between  the  Tec-Dara  and DPd/DVd groups ( Table 22 ). The incidence of serious TEAEs was higher in the Tec-Dara group (TecDara: 70.7%, control: 62.4%). The incidence of discontinuation of all study treatment due to TEAEs was low and comparable in both treatment groups (Tec-Dara: 4.6%; control: 5.5%).

Of note, TEAE rates were not adjusted for treatment duration (median treatment duration with TecDara [32.4 months], which was twice as long as that of DPd/DVd [16.1 months].

## Most commonly reported TEAEs

At least 1 TEAE was reported for 100.0% of participants in both the Tec-Dara and control groups ( Table 23 ). The most commonly reported TEAEs in the Tec-Dara group were consistent with the most commonly reported TEAEs for teclistamab monotherapy and daratumumab monotherapy.

## Adverse Drug Reactions

Adverse drug reactions are AEs considered to be reasonably associated with the use of teclistamab based on a comprehensive assessment of available AE information. Previously established ADRs were based on teclistamab monotherapy in MajesTEC-1. Because MajesTEC-3 was a randomised study with a comparator group, it provided sufficient evidence for the confirmative determination of ADRs for teclistamab as detailed below. Furthermore daratumumab was a component of both treatment groups (Tec-Dara vs DPd/DVd), and thus, its attribution to any TEAEs was also evaluated in the determination of ADRs for teclistamab.

The applicant's medical experts evaluated data using the definition of ADR from the ICH E6(R3) Guideline for Good Clinical Practice. The determination of ADRs is based on the combined assessment of incidence, overall trends, biological plausibility, medical judgment, and individual case review, as necessary.

ADR assessment for MajesTEC-3 was based on all TEAEs and laboratory abnormalities reported in the 283 participants in the Safety Analysis Set in the Tec-Dara group. The ADR analysis of TEAEs was conducted in a stepwise manner according to the following criteria:

- TEAEs as individual preferred terms from MajesTEC-3 were first analysed based on the below:
- TEAEs reported in ≥ 10% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and reported at ≥ 5% higher incidence compared to the control group (DPd/DVd).
- Serious TEAEs reported in ≥ 2% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and reported at ≥ 2% higher incidence compared to the control group (DPd/DVd).
- TEAEs  were  additionally  evaluated  in  the  context  of  a  potential  plausible  biological  or pharmacological association  with  teclistamab  or  daratumumab  or  as  medically  significant events with a high probability that they could be associated with teclistamab or daratumumab, regardless of frequency.
- All Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities reported in ≥ 10% of participants in the Tec-Dara group  were  included.  Additionally,  other  clinically  important  Grade  3  or  4  laboratory abnormalities reported in ≥ 2% and up to 10% of participants in the Tec-Dara group, and reported  at ≥ 1% higher  incidence  compared  to  the  control  group  (DPd/DVd)  were  also included.

## New adverse drug reactions

A review of safety data in the MajesTEC-3 study identified 1 new ADR (weight decreased) compared with MajesTEC-1 teclistamab monotherapy as described below.

Weight decreased was reported in ≥ 10% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and the difference in the incidence in the Tec-Dara group was ≥ 5% higher than the control group (Tec-Dara: 20%; control: 7%) ( Table 25 ). Thus, it met the pre-defined ADR criteria. Nearly all events in the Tec-Dara group were low grade (grade 1 or 2 in 49 of 57 [86.0%] participants who experienced weight decreased) and the incidence of grade 3 weight decreased was low (Tec-Dara: 2.8%; control: 1.4%). No grade 4 or 5 events nor serious events were reported. One participant in the Tec-Dara group discontinued teclistamab treatment due to grade 3 weight decreased but remained on daratumumab monotherapy treatment; the event subsequently resolved. More than half of the participants had events that were resolved/resolving at the time of the CCO (any grade: 34 of 57 participants [~60%]) and nearly all participants with grade 3 events had an outcome of resolved/resolving at the time of the CCO (7 of 8 participants). Overall, events of weight decreased were determined to be tolerable and manageable by standard of care. No additional risk minimization measures were required.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Rhinorrhoea also met the ADR criterion of TEAE(s) that occurred in ≥ 10% of participants and at ≥ 5% higher incidence in the Tec-Dara group as compared with the control group (Tec-Dara: n=33/283, 11.7%; control: n=19/290, 6.6%). However, a review of the 33 participants who reported a TEAE of rhinorrhoea revealed concurrent upper respiratory tract infections (ie, 14 days before or 7 days after onset  of  rhinorrhoea)  in  13  cases,  suggesting  rhinorrhoea  is  most  likely  a  symptom  of  upper respiratory tract infection (already an ADR). Excluding these 13 cases, the adjusted incidence of rhinorrhoea in the Tec-Dara group is 7.1% (n=20/283), which would no longer meet the ADR criteria and thus rhinorrhoea is not considered a new ADR for teclistamab.

The PT of blood lactate dehydrogenase increased met the ADR criterion of TEAE(s) that occurred in ≥ 10% of participants and at a ≥ 5% difference higher incidence in the Tec-Dara group as compared with the control group (Tec-Dara: 11.3%; control: 3.8%). However, blood lactate dehydrogenase increased is a laboratory abnormality and did not meet the ADR determination criterion for other clinically important grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities occurring in ≥ 2% to 10% of participants in the Tec-Dara group, and reported at ≥ 1% higher incidence compared to the control group as there were no grade 3 or 4 events in Tec-Dara group. The finding of elevated blood lactate dehydrogenase in  multiple myeloma patients has confounding factors particularly in patients with a high tumour burden and increased cellular lysis, common after the initiation of anti-myeloma therapy. As such, blood lactate dehydrogenase increased is not considered an ADR for teclistamab.

## Pooled teclistamab ADR Database

Given the consistency of the safety data from the Study MajesTEC-3 with the known safety profile of teclistamab  from  MajesTEC-1  monotherapy,  and  comparable  patient  population  across  both  the studies, the MAH considers that these data could be combined to obtain ADR frequencies from a pooled  safety  data  set.  In  addition,  a  second  Phase  3  study  (MajesTEC-9)  which  was  recently submitted  to  the  EMA  (EMA/VR/0000336274),  provides  additional  safety  data  on  teclistamab monotherapy (n=291). No new ADRs were identified following detailed review of these data; data from MajesTEC-9 were then pooled with those from MajesTEC-3 and MajesTEC-1. ( Table 29 ).

7 Hypogammaglobulinaemia includes patients with adverse events of hypogammaglobulinaemia, hyperglobulinaemia, immunoglobulins decreased; and/or patients with laboratory IgG levels below 400 mg/dL following treatment with TECVAYLI.

8 Hypertension includes Blood pressure increased, Essential hypertension, Hypertension,

Hypertensive crisis, Mean arterial pressure increased and Systolic hypertension.

9 Cough includes Allergic cough, Cough, Productive cough and Upper-airway cough syndrome.

10 Dyspnoea includes Acute respiratory failure, Dyspnoea, Dyspnoea exertional and Respiratory failure.

11 Diarrhoea includes Bacterial diarrhoea, Diarrhoea and Viral diarrhoea.

12 Abdominal pain includes Abdominal discomfort, Abdominal pain, Abdominal pain lower, Abdominal pain upper, Epigastric discomfort and Gastrointestinal pain.

13 Musculoskeletal pain includes Arthralgia, Back pain, Bone pain, Musculoskeletal chest pain, Musculoskeletal pain, Myalgia, Neck pain, Pain in extremity and Sacral pain.

14 Injection site reaction includes Administration site pruritus, Administration site reaction, Application site erythema, Injection site bruising, Injection site cellulitis, Injection site discolouration, Injection site discomfort, Injection site eczema, Injection site erythema, Injection site haematoma, Injection site hyperaesthesia, Injection site induration, Injection site inflammation, Injection site irritation, Injection site oedema, Injection site pain, Injection site paraesthesia, Injection site pruritus, Injection site rash, Injection site reaction, Injection site swelling, Injection site vesicles and Injection site warmth.

15 Fatigue includes Asthenia, Fatigue and Malaise.

16 Pain includes Axillary pain, Breast pain, Cancer pain, Chest pain, Ear pain, Eye pain, Facial pain, Flank pain, Gingival pain, Groin pain, Laryngeal pain, Non-cardiac chest pain, Oesophageal pain, Oral pain, Oropharyngeal pain, Pain, Pain in jaw, Pain of skin, Pelvic pain, Perineal pain, Periorbital pain, Sinus pain, Spinal pain, Testicular pain, Toothache and Tumour pain.

17 Oedema includes Eyelid oedema, Face oedema, Localised oedema, oedema, oedema peripheral, Periorbital oedema, Peripheral swelling, Skin oedema, Swelling, Swelling face and Testicular swelling.

18 Transaminase elevation includes Alanine aminotransferase increased, Aspartate aminotransferase, Aspartate aminotransferase increased, Hypertransaminasaemia and Transaminase increased.

* Based on post-marketing experience.

## Serious adverse events/deaths/other significant events

## Serious treatment-emergent adverse events

The incidence of serious TEAEs was higher in the Tec-Dara group (Tec-Dara: 200 [70.7%]; control: 181 [62.4%]) ( Table 30 ), with the events most commonly occurring within the SOC of Infections and Infestations (Tec-Dara: 154 [54.4%]; control: 121 [41.7%]).

Events of CRS and infections are further described in detail in the section Adverse Events of Clinical Interest.

## Deaths

A total of 45 participants (15.9%) in the Tec-Dara group and 96 participants (33.1%) in the control group died after the start of study treatment as of the clinical cut-off, including 10 participants in the Tec-Dara group and 15 participants in the control group who died within 30 days of the last dose of study treatment ( Table 31 ).

Deaths  due  to  'other'  causes,  defined  as  those  where  the  primary  cause  of  death  was  neither progressive disease nor a TEAE, were reported for 4.2% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and 6.9%  of  participants  in  the  control  group.  Most  deaths  categorized  as  'other'  occurred  after

Among the 40 participants who experienced grade 5 TEAEs, 2 had grade 5 events of general physical health deterioration and 1 had plasma cell leukaemia. The investigator attributed disease progression as the cause of death for all 3 participants.

Grade 5 TEAEs of infection (TEAEs reported in the Infections and Infestations SOC) were reported with a higher incidence in the Tec-Dara group compared to the control group:

- Tec-Dara: 13 participants (4.6%), the majority of whom had sepsis or pneumonia, including 3 fatal COVID-19 infections.
- Control: 4 participants (1.4%), no fatal COVID-19 infections.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Of the 13 fatal infections in the Tec-Dara group, 12 occurred within the first 6 months of treatment initiation.  Notably,  all  12  deaths  took  place  before  the  implementation  of  Amendment  6  and Amendment  7,  which  included  enhanced  guidance  on  immunoglobulin  replacement  therapy  and infection prophylaxis and management, including specific guidance on the management of COVID19 infections. Most of them (9 participants) did not receive any immunoglobulin replacement therapy. After the amendments, only 1 fatal infection was reported in the Tec-Dara group.

Additionally, 1 participant in the Tec-Dara group died within the first 6 months of treatment initiation due to a TEAE of fulminant hepatitis resulting from severe COVID-19 infection.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The most common symptom of CRS was fever, which was reported in all participants with CRS. Most participants received paracetamol or tocilizumab as supportive treatment for CRS. Corticosteroids were used in 2.8% of participants. Vasopressor use was reported in 1 participant with grade 1 CRS: the  participant  developed  fever  and  diarrhoea,  which  were  considered  symptoms  of  CRS.  The diarrhoea was managed with racecadotril and was summarized as vasopressor use.

Grade 2 CRS tended to occur after early doses of teclistamab, with the highest frequency after step - up dose 1 (8.1%); grade 2 CRS occurred less frequently after step-up dose 2 (6.0%) or the first treatment  dose  (2.5%),  and  it  was  rare  for  subsequent  doses  ( Figure  16 ).  There  were  no occurrences of grade 2 CRS beyond cycle 1. Among all the participants who developed grade 2 CRS, only 1.8% experienced a subsequent event of grade 2 CRS ( Table 33 ).

CRS was reported following a repeat step-up dose in 7 participants (2.5%), and all of these CRS events  were  grade  1.  Among  these  cases,  5  occurred  before  the  participant  received  the  first treatment dose of teclistamab.

In the Tec-Dara group, 14 participants were monitored in the outpatient setting for at least 1 dose of teclistamab through the first treatment dose. Outpatient monitoring was most frequently for the treatment dose of 1.5 mg/kg. Of these 14 participants, 7 experienced CRS (6 participants with grade 1 events and 1 participant with a grade 2 event). The CRS was managed in the outpatient setting for 4 participants.

## Neurological adverse events

Any TEAE reported in either the Nervous System Disorder SOC or the Psychiatric Disorders SOC was identified as a neurological AE. Additionally, any event captured by Standardised MedDRA Query in the  grouped  terms  of  encephalopathy,  peripheral  neuropathy,  and  motor  dysfunction  were considered neurological AEs and presented in these grouped terms.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Any grade neurological adverse event

Neurological AEs occurred in 60.1% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and 62.8% of participants in the control group, and most were of low grade.

Grouped terms for neurological AEs were reported with the following incidence and all occurred at a higher  frequency  in  the  control  group  vs  the  Tec-Dara  group:  peripheral  neuropathy  (Tec-Dara: 16.3%; control: 28.3%), motor dysfunction (Tec-Dara: 14.8%; control: 26.9%), encephalopathy (Tec-Dara: 9.9%; control: 15.2%).

Among  the  terms  without  grouping,  the  most  frequently  reported  neurological  AEs  ( ≥ 20%  of participants  in  either  treatment  group)  by  preferred  term  were:  headache  (Tec-Dara:  25.8%; control: 11.7%) and insomnia (Tec-Dara: 14.8%; control: 21.7%).

## Grade 3 or 4 neurological adverse event

Grade 3 or 4 neurological AEs occurred in 7.8% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and 11.7% of participants in the control group. Grouped terms for grade 3 or 4 neurological AEs were reported at a similar rate in both groups, with the following incidences: peripheral neuropathy (Tec-Dara: 0.7%; control: 0.7%), motor dysfunction (Tec-Dara: no events reported; control: 1.4%), encephalopathy (Tec-Dara: 0.4%; control: 1.7%).

Among the terms without grouping, the most frequently reported AE by preferred terms ( ≥ 2.0% in either treatment group) for grade 3 or 4 neurological AEs were syncope (Tec-Dara: 2.5%; control: 4.1%) and insomnia (Tec-Dara: no events reported; control: 3.4%).

## Neurotoxicity

Additionally, teclistamab-related neurotoxicity events were further identified via a CRF tick box which was checked by the investigator for 17.3% of participants in the Tec - Dara group ( Table 34 ).

## Immune Effector Cell-Associated Neurotoxicity Syndrome (ICANS)

ICANS was reported for 3 participants in the Tec-Dara group (1.1%; 2 participants with grade 1 events and 1 participant with a grade 4 event). The ICANS events occurred during step-up dose 1 (1 participant)  and  the  first  treatment  dose  (2  participants).  ICANS  was  not  reported  for  any participants after repeat step-up doses. No participants had multiple ICANS events. All events had recovered.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Grade 4 ICANS occurred in 1 participant after receiving the first treatment dose of teclistamab. At the time of the event, the immune Effector Cell Encephalopathy (ICE) score was 0, and the participant required vigorous or repetitive tactile stimuli to arouse. Intubation and mechanical ventilation was not indicated since the participant was breathing without assistance and able to protect their airway. The ICANS resolved in less than 12 hours. The participant discontinued teclistamab due to this event.

## Cytopenic adverse events

Incidences of cytopenic TEAEs were similar between the Tec - Dara and control groups: neutropenia: Tec - Dara, 78.4%; control, 82.8%, anaemia: Tec - Dara, 39.2%; control, 35.5%, thrombocytopenia: Tec - Dara, 36.4%; control, 43.4%, lymphopenia: Tec - Dara, 22.3%; control, 17.2%.

Most events in both treatment groups were grade 3 or 4 (Tec-Dara: 82.3% of participants; control: 86.9% of participants). New onset of grade ≥ 3 cytopenic TEAEs in the Tec-Dara group occurred most commonly during the first 6 months of treatment and decreased over time.

Transfusions  (whole  blood,  packed  red  blood  cells,  fresh  frozen  plasma,  or  platelets)  were administered on-study to 25.1% of participants in the Tec - Dara group and 19.0% of participants in the control group.

## Haemorrhage events

Haemorrhagic events were reported for 12.7% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and 14.5% of participants in the control group. Grade 3 or 4 events were reported for 2.1% of participants in the Tec-Dara group and 1.0% of participants in the control group. No grade 5 events were reported in either treatment group.

## Infection

## Any grade infection

At least 1 infection of any grade was reported in 273 participants (96.5%) in the Tec-Dara group and in  244 participants  (84.1%) in  the  control  group.  New-onset  infections  occurred  most  commonly during the first 6 months and decreased over time.

Pathogen unspecified infections (high-level terms) with a ≥ 10% incidence in the Tec-Dara group were upper respiratory tract infections (185 participants, 65.4%), lower respiratory tract and lung infections  (104 participants,  36.7%), urinary tract infections (36 participants, 12.7%), abdominal and gastrointestinal infections (34 participants, 12%), and infections NEC (29 participants, 10.2%).

In pathogen-specified infections, viral infectious disorders were observed in 215 participants (76%) in  the  Tec-Dara  group,  with  COVID-19  being  the  most  common  viral  infection  (124 participants, 43.8%).  Bacterial  and  fungal  infectious  disorders  occurred  in  105 participants  (37.1%)  and  in 40 participants (14.1%), respectively.

## Grade ≥ 3 infections

At least 1 grade ≥ 3 infection was reported in 153 participants (54.1%) in the Tec-Dara group and 126 participants  (43.4%)  in  the  control  group.  New-onset  grade ≥ 3  infections  occurred  most commonly during the first 6 months of treatment and decreased over time ( Figure 17 ). No grade 5 events reported in either treatment group.

Grade ≥ 3 pathogen unspecified infections (high-level terms) with a ≥ 5% incidence in the Tec-Dara group were lower respiratory tract and lung infections (53 participants, 18.7%) and upper respiratory tract infections (20 participants, 7.1%).

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In  grade ≥ 3 pathogen-specified infections by high-level grouped terms, viral infectious disorders were observed in 83 participants (29.3%) in the Tec-Dara group, with COVID-19 pneumonia being the most common grade ≥ 3 viral infection (32 participants, 11.3%). Grade ≥ 3 bacterial and fungal infections disorders occurred in 54 participants (19.1%) and in 15 participants (5.3%), respectively.

## Opportunistic infections

At least 1 opportunistic infection was reported in 74 participants (26.1%) in the Tec-Dara group and in 38 participants (13.1%) in the control group. New-onset opportunistic infections occurred most commonly during the first 6 months of Tec-Dara treatment and decreased over time. Incidences for selected opportunistic infections were as follows:

PML: no events reported in either treatment group

HBV reactivation: Tec - Dara 3 (2.5%); control 3 (1.0%)

CMV: Tec - Dara 27 (9.5%); control 8 (2.8%)

Herpes viral infection: Tec - Dara 26 (9.2%); control 9 (3.1%)

## COVID-19

This study was initiated and was conducted during the COVID-19 pandemic. A past medical history of COVID-19 prior to enrolment was reported in 21.3% of participants (Tec-Dara: 20.3%; control: 22.3%). Additionally, 13.6% of participants in the Tec-Dara group had a prior history of COVID-19 pneumonia, compared to 3% in the control group.

Key results for COVID-19 were as follows:

Vaccination for COVID-19 before first study dose: Tec - Dara 242 (85.5%); control 240 (82.8%)

Any treatment-emergent COVID-19: Tec - Dara 130 (45.9%); control 106 (36.6%)

Grade 3 or 4 COVID-19 (maximum toxicity grade): Tec - Dara 40 (14.1%); control 13 (4.5%)

Grade 5 COVID-19: Tec - Dara 3 (1.1%); control 0

One additional death in the Tec-Dara group was also related to COVID-19 infection. This participant died due to a TEAE of fulminant hepatitis resulting from severe COVID-19 infection.

## Hypogammaglobulinemia

Hypogammaglobulinemia (either a TEAE or a post-baseline IgG value &lt;400 mg/dL) was reported in 239 participants (84.5%) in the Tec-Dara group and in 175 participants (60.3%) in the control group.

Grade 3 or 4 hypogammaglobulinemia were reported more frequently in the Tec-Dara arm compared to control group (5.7% vs. 1.4%).

IVIg/SCIg was received by 247 participants (87.3%) in the Tec-Dara group and by 130 participants (44.8%) in the control group.

## Hepatotoxicity

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Hepatotoxicity events, primarily elevated liver enzymes, were reported in 86 participants (30.4%) in the  Tec-Dara  group  and  37 participants  (12.8%)  in  the  control  group.  No  participants  in  either treatment  group  discontinued  study  treatment  due  to  hepatotoxicity  events.  Most  cases  of hepatotoxicity events were mild or moderate in severity. Grade 3 or 4 maximum toxicity events were reported in 22 participants (7.8%) in the Tec-Dara group and 4 participants (1.4%) in the control group. One grade 5 event was reported in each treatment group (hepatitis fulminant in a participant with severe COVID-19 infection in the Tec-Dara group and hepatic failure of unknown cause in the control group).

Laboratory abnormalities that worsened from baseline are summarized in the section of Laboratory findings below. Grade 3 or 4 chemistry laboratory abnormalities related to liver function tests were infrequent in both treatment groups.

Three participants met laboratory criteria for potential Hy's law in the Tec-Dara group. However, upon  review  of  the  clinical  context,  all  cases  had  alternative  explanations  for  the  elevated transaminases and bilirubin: one participant experienced liver enzyme elevation as a symptom of CRS; another had recent CMV viremia; and the third had abnormal liver function tests due to biliary obstruction  following  laparoscopic  cholecystectomy.  Therefore,  no  participants  met  the  Hy's  law criteria.

## Second primary malignancy

Second primary  malignancies  occurred  in  35 participants  (12.4%)  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  and  in 25 participants (8.6%) in the control group and were most commonly cutaneous/non-invasive. The incidence rates of haematologic and solid malignancies were balanced between the treatment groups.

The incidence by type of second primary malignancy was as follows:

Cutaneous/non-invasive: Tec - Dara 23 (8.1%); control, 13 (4.5%)

Haematologic: Tec - Dara 8 (2.8%); control 4 (1.4%)

Solid malignancy: Tec - Dara 6 (2.1%); control 10 (3.4%)

## Injection-site Reactions

At least 1 injection-site reaction related to teclistamab was reported for 38.5% of participants in the Tec-Dara  group.  All  of  the  reported  injection-site  reactions  were  grade  1  or  2.  No  participants discontinued due to ISRs.

## Systemic administration-related reactions

At least 1 sARR related to teclistamab was reported for 3.2% of participants in the Tec-Dara group; these events were low grade and usually occurred early during the initiation of treatment.

The incidences of sARRs was similar between the 2 treatment groups and were mostly grade 1 or 2 in severity. The majority of sARRs occurred after the first dose of daratumumab.

## Immune-mediated adverse events

Immune-mediated AEs were of low incidence and reported for 9 participants (3.2%) in the Tec-Dara group  and  1 participant  (0.3%)  in  the  control  group.  Grade  3  or  4  immune-mediated  AEs  were reported for 4 participants (1.4%) in the Tec-Dara group (adrenal insufficiency/Addison's disease for 3 participants and immune-mediated lung disease for 1 participant) and 1 participant (0.3%) in the control group (interstitial lung disease).

## Tumour lysis syndrome

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Treatment-emergent  TLS  was  reported  for  2 participants  (0.7%)  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  and  no participants in the control group. Neither participant discontinued study treatment due to TLS.

## Laboratory findings

MajesTEC-3 Laboratory Abnormalities ADR determination criteria, included all Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities reported in ≥ 10% of participants in the Tec -Dara group, as well as other clinically important Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities reported in ≥ 2% and up to 10% of participants in the TecDara group, and reported at ≥ 1% higher incidence compared to the control group (DPd/DVd). As a result, the following laboratory abnormality values did not meet the criteria: hypofibrinogenemia (any grade: 0.4%, grade 3/4:0%), hypocalcaemia (any grade: 13%, Grade 3/4: 1.8%), hypoglycaemia (any grade: 1.4%, grade 3/4: 0%), hypoalbuminemia (any grade: 8%, Grade 3/4: 0.4%), hypomagnesaemia (all grade: 3.9%, grade 3/4: 0%), blood creatinine increased (any grade: 7%, grade 3/4: 0.4%), blood alkaline phosphatase increased (any grade: 8%, grade 3/4: 0%), activated partial thromboplastin time prolonged (all grade: 0.4%, grade 3/4: 0%) and International Normalized Ratio increased (any grade: 0.7%, grade 3/4: 0%).

Laboratory  abnormalities  that  worsened  from  baseline  are  summarized  in Table 37 .  Cytopenia, hypogammaglobulinemia and hepatotoxicity TEAEs are discussed in the section Adverse Events of Clinical Interest above.

## Vital signs

No clinically meaningful differences in vital signs were noted between the treatment groups, except for greater weight loss from baseline in the Tec-Dara group compared to control: maximum weight

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

loss of 5- &lt;10% (Tec-Dara: 77 participants [27.2%]; control: 56 participants [19.3%]); 10-&lt;20% (Tec-Dara:  69 participants  [24.4%];  control:  25 participants  [8.6%]);  and ≥ 20%  (Tec-Dara: 13 participants [4.6%; control: 5 participants [1.7%]).

## Immunogenicity

## Teclistamab

Among  participants  in  the  Tec-Dara  treatment  group,  270 participants  were  included  in  the teclistamab immunogenicity evaluable analysis set, and 236 and 71 participants, respectively, had evaluable data at ≥ 6 months and ≥ 1 year after the first dose of teclistamab. The overall incidence of participants with treatment-emergent antibodies to teclistamab in the Tec-Dara combination was 2 out of 270 or &lt;1% of the population. This observation is consistent with the anticipated overall low (&lt;1%) ADA incidence for teclistamab. One participant had a titer of 1:11.25 at Cycle 12 Day 1 (last available sample) and was negative for neutralizing antibodies to teclistamab. The other participant had a titer of 1:45 at a Cycle 1 Day 15 unscheduled visit and was positive for neutralizing antibodies to teclistamab.

## Daratumumab

Among the participants in the Tec-Dara group, 268 participants were included in the daratumumab immunogenicity  evaluable  analysis  set.  One  participant  (0.4%)  was  identified  as  positive  for antibodies to daratumumab with a titer of 1:384 at the EOT visit; the neutralizing antibody status is not available yet due to ongoing sample analysis. In the control group, 280 participants were included in daratumumab immunogenicity evaluable analysis set. One participant was treatment boosted for anti-daratumumab antibodies with a titer of 1:96 at C3D1 compared to 1:24 at baseline and was negative for neutralizing antibodies. The anti-daratumumab antibody incidence is the same between the Tec-Dara group and control group and is consistent with the overall low (&lt;1%) ADA incidence rate observed in previous daratumumab subcutaneous studies.

## Safety in special populations

## Intrinsic Factors

Analyses were performed to evaluate the safety of teclistamab in the MajesTEC-3 among subgroups defined by intrinsic factors of age group, sex, race, baseline renal function, and baseline hepatic function. For the Tec-Dara group, the overall safety profile, the most commonly reported TEAEs, grade 3 or 4 TEAEs, and serious TEAEs for Tec-Dara were generally similar across all subgroups (data not shown).

## Age

The distribution of participants in &lt;65 years, 65 to &lt;75 years, and ≥ 75 years subgroups was as follows in the MajesTEC-3 study:

&lt;65 years: Tec-Dara: 147 (50.5%); control: 160 (54.1%)

65 years to &lt;75 years: Tec-Dara: 113 (38.8%); control: 111 (37.5%)

≥ 75 years: Tec-Dara: 31 (10.7%); control: 25 (8.4%)

The overall incidence of TEAEs, grade 3 or 4 TEAEs, and serious TEAEs for Tec-Dara were generally similar across the age subgroups. TEAEs with a fatal outcome were more frequent in participants aged ≥ 75 years, but overall rates were similar between treatment groups (13.3% in Tec-Dara vs.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

12.5% in controls). TEAEs leading to discontinuation of all study treatment were also higher in the ≥ 75-year subgroup, with comparable overall rates between Tec-Dara (10%) and control (12.5%). Given  the  small  number  of  participants  in  the ≥ 75-year  subgroup,  these  findings  should  be interpreted with caution. Overall, no safety signals were detected across age subgroups.

## Safety related to drug-drug interactions and other interactions

No drug-drug interaction studies were submitted with teclistamab.

## Discontinuation due to adverse events

## Discontinuations and/or dose modifications due to adverse events

The incidence of discontinuation of all study treatment due to TEAEs was low and similar across treatment groups (Tec-Dara: 4.6%; control: 5.5%) (Table 9.1.13). The most commonly reported TEAEs (reported in ≥ 2 participants) leading to discontinuation of all study treatment in the Tec-Dara group were asthenia, cough, diarrhoea, and pneumonia (reported in 2 participants each). These were also the most commonly reported TEAEs leading to discontinuation of teclistamab or discontinuation of daratumumab.

## Cycle delay and dose modification

The incidence of cycle delay or dose modification due to TEAEs was similar across treatment groups (Tec-Dara: 92.9%; control: 94.1%). TEAEs leading to cycle delay or dose modification were most commonly reported ( ≥ 30% of participants in either treatment group) within the SOCs of Infections and Infestations (Tec-Dara: 78.1%; control: 62.8%) and Blood and Lymphatic System Disorders (Tec-Dara: 58.0%; control:70.0%).

TEAEs leading to dose reductions for teclistamab were most frequently reported ( ≥ 5% of participants) within the SOCs of Infections and Infestations (21.2%) and Blood and Lymphatic System Disorders (7.4%).

TEAEs leading to dose interruption in the Tec-Dara group were most frequently reported ( ≥ 10% of participants) within the SOCs of Infections and Infestations (39.2%), Blood and Lymphatic System Disorders (32.2%), and Immune System Disorders (13.4%).

## Safety data from TriMM-2

Sixty-one participants in the RP2D cohorts (i.e., treated with 1.5 mg/kg weekly or 3.0 mg/kg Q2W teclistamab) received a dose of any study treatment on or before the CCO of 31 January 2024 and were included in the Safety Analysis Set for this population.

Almost all participants (93.4%) experienced Grade 3 or 4 TEAEs. These events were most frequently reported  in  the  SOCs  of  Blood  and  Lymphatic  System  Disorders  (78.7%)  and  Infection  and Infestations (52.5%). No Grade ≥ 3 CRS or ICANS were reported. As of the CCO, 12 participants (19.7%) died, 7 (11.5%) of these due to TEAEs and 5 (8.2%) due to disease progression.

Among the 7 participants who died due to TEAEs, 5 died due to fatal infections: 2 participants with COVID-19 and 1 participant each with pseudomonal sepsis, enterococcal sepsis, and septic shock. No DLTs were reported.

Serious TEAEs were reported for 80.3% of participants, most frequently CRS (18.0%), followed by COVID-19 (16.4%), and pneumonia (11.5%). Overall, 13.1% of participants experienced TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation of both study drugs.
