<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Post marketing experience

There is currently no postmarketing experience with teclistamab in combination with daratumumab.

## 2.5.1. Discussion on clinical safety

The safety analysis set of the main study MajesTEC-3 comprised 573 participants (Tec-Dara: n=283; control:  n=290)  who  received  any  part  of  the  study  treatment.  The  cut-off  date  for  the  safety database is based on the first protocol - specified interim analysis for the primary endpoint (PFS) that was to be performed after approximately 75% of the total planned PFS events were observed.

The supportive safety data is derived from the RP2D cohorts the Study TriMM-2 and consists of two RP2D cohorts of phase 1 study including 61 participants.

## Main study: MajesTEC-3

The median duration of treatment in the participants treated with Tec-Dara was twice as long as in the  control  group  (32.4 months  vs.  16.1 months,  respectively).  At  least  24 months  on  study treatment  was  reached  by  73.1%  of  the  participants  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  and  39.7%  of  the participants  in  the  control  group.  Despite  the  longer  treatment  duration  in  the  Tec-Dara  group, discontinuation of the study treatment was more frequent in the control group (29.0% in the TecDara group vs. 71.7% in controls) which was mainly due to progressive disease (7.4% in Tec-Dara group, 57.9% in controls).

## Overall safety profile

At least one TEAE was reported in all participants in both groups. The most reported TEAEs were in the SOC of Infections and infestations (96.5% in the Tec-Dara group and 84.1% in the controls). Neutropenia and lymphopenia were reported frequently in both groups (in approximately 80% and 20% of the participants, respectively) and hypogammaglobulinemia was a frequent finding especially in the Tec-Dara group (68% of the participants).

Among the participants treated with the combination of teclistamab and daratumumab, the frequency of CRS of any grade is similar with that of the known safety profile of teclistamab monotherapy.

## Treatment-emergent grade 3 or 4 adverse events

The  total  number  of  participants  with  at  least  one  grade  3  or  4  TEAE  was  similar  between  the treatment groups (95% in the Tec-Dara group and 96% in controls). Approximately 75% of the participants  in  both  groups  experienced  grade  &gt;3  neutropenia  which  was  the  most  frequently reported grade 3 or 4 TEAE. Grade 3 or 4 lymphopenia and hypogammaglobulinemia were reported more frequently in the Tec-Dara arm compared to control group (20.8% and 5.7% vs. 11% and 1.4%, respectively). Cytopenias are not an unexpected finding in patients with multiple myeloma with poor bone marrow reserve caused by prior treatments or due to active disease with plasma cell involvement in the bone marrow. However, as most haematological toxicities can be managed with teclistamab dose delays, cytopenias could also be related to treatment with BCMA-targeting T-cellengaging bispecific antibodies even though the specific mechanism is unknown.

Grade &gt;3 infections were more frequent in the Tec-Dara group (54.1% in Tec-Dara vs. 43.4% in controls). The study was conducted during and shortly after the COVID-19 pandemia and grade &gt;3 COVID-19 infections (including COVID-19 pneumonia) were reported with higher frequencies in TecDara group than in controls (17.3% vs. 4.5%). On the contrary, no difference in the incidences of

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

grade 3 or 4 non-COVID-19 pneumonias between the groups was detected.

## Serious treatment-emergent adverse events

Serious  AEs,  mainly  infections,  were  common  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  (54%).  The  most  common serious infectious AEs included pneumonia, COVID-19 pneumonia and COVID-19. Serious pneumonic events,  with  causative  pathogen  other  than  COVID-19,  occurred  approximately  with  similar frequencies in the Tec-Dara group and in controls (16.6 vs. 13.1%, respectively). The difference in the incidences of serious COVID-19 events (including COVID-19 pneumonia) was clear between the groups (18.0% in the Tec-Dara group vs. 4.9% in controls).

No ≥ grade  3  CRS  events  were  reported,  but  serious  CRS  events  were  reported  in  9.5%  of  the participants in the Tec-Dara group, which reflects the need for hospitalisation of patients even with lower grades of CRS.

## Discontinuations and/or dose modifications due to adverse events

The incidence of discontinuation of all study treatment was low and comparable in both treatment groups  (4.6%  in  Tec-Dara  and  in  5.5%  in  control  group).  The  most  common  reasons  for discontinuation  of  all  study  treatment  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  (asthenia,  cough,  diarrhoea,  and pneumonia,  in  2  participants  each),  were  also  the  most  common  reasons  for  discontinuation  of teclistamab or daratumumab.

Cycle  delays  and  dose  modifications  due  to  AEs  were  common  in  both  groups  (&gt;90%  of  the participants).

## Proposal for adverse reactions

The MAH has provided a detailed description of the used methodology in ADR determination to justify the addition of one new ADR (weight decreased) which is considered acceptable.

Based on the MAH's justification for ADR determination in laboratory abnormalities, the deletion of the following ADRs are considered acceptable: hypofibrinogenemia, hypocalcaemia, hypercalcaemia, hypoglycaemia,  hypoalbuminemia,  hypomagnesaemia,  blood  creatinine  increased,  blood  alkaline phosphatase  increased, Activated Partial Thromboplastin  Time  prolonged,  and  International Normalized Ratio increased.

Frequency of ADRs is based on pooled safety data from MajesTEC-1 (N=165), MajesTEC-3 (N=283) and MajesTEC-9 (N=291, currently under assessment through the ongoing variation EMA/VR/0000336274) studies for the SmPC section 4.8.

## Deaths

Disease progression leading to death occurred more frequently in the control group (20.3%) than in the  Tec-Dara  group  (4.6%).  The  incidence  of  fatal  TEAEs  was  slightly  higher  in  the  participants treated with teclistamab in combination with daratumumab compared to that of controls (7.1% vs 5.9%, respectively).  Of  the  20  fatal  TEAEs  in  the  Tec-Dara  group,  12  (4.1%)  were  assessed  as treatment-related by the investigator.

Treatment-emergent fatal infectious AEs were reported with a higher incidence in the Tec-Dara group than in the control group (13 participants [4.6%] vs. 4 participants [1.4%], respectively). The fatal infections included septicaemia (5), pneumonia (3), COVID-19 infections (3), enteroviral myocarditis (1) and West Nile viral infection (1). Except for one fatal septic shock at day 807, all fatal infectious TEAEs occurred within the first 6 months of treatment initiation. However, in both treatment groups, the incidence of early death (within 60 days of first study treatment dose) due to TEAE was low which may be explained by the prophylactic antibiotics used during the early weeks of the study.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The MAH amended the clinical study protocol after fatal infections were observed in 8/31 participants treated with fixed dose teclistamab in combination with daratumumab in the phase 1 study TriMM2.  The  amendment  enhanced  the  guidance  regarding  immunoglobulin  replacement,  and  247 participants  (87.3%)  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  and  130  participants  (44.8%)  in  the  control  group received at least 1 dose of IV or SC immunoglobulin treatment. Nine out of 13 participants with fatal outcome  had  not  received  immunoglobulin  replacement  therapy.  The  MAH  indicated  that  after implementation  of  the  recommendation  for  immunoglobulin  maintenance  treatment  to  ensure participants to maintain serum IgG levels ≥400 mg/dL, only one fatal infection was reported in the Tec-Dara group. However, the small number of participants initiating the treatment after this protocol amendment  limits  the  possibility  to  evaluate  the  effect  of  IVIg  maintenance  treatment  on  the incidence of severe or fatal infections as only 6 participants initiated Tec-Dara after this protocol amendment. Among the participants who started Tec-Dara prior to this amendment, 145 participants had been treated for at least for 6 months, which is the most critical time period for infections. Grade 3-4  infections  or  fatal  infections  were  observed  in  37.9%  and  4.3%  of  these  participants, respectively. Of the 6 participants who started Tec-Dara after PA6, 5 received IVIg/SCIg maintenance treatment, and none experienced grade &gt;3 TE infections.

The current SmPC section 4.4 Special warnings and precautions for use of teclistamab includes the recommendation  to  follow  serum  immunoglobulin  levels  and  to  administer  immunoglobulin maintenance treatment according to local guidelines. The proposed addition of S-IgG threshold level of &gt;400 mg/dl is aligned with the MajesTEC-3 protocol. Considering the high incidence of severe infections in the patients treated with the combination therapy of teclistamab and daratumumab the addition of this recommendation to maintain serum IgG levels ≥400 mg/dL is considered acceptable.

## Adverse Events of Clinical Interests (AECI)

## Cytokine release syndrome

The  frequency  and  severity  of  CRS  reported  in  the  combination  treatment  of  teclistamab  and daratumumab  was  similar  with  those  reported  priorly  in  the  single-arm  trial  of  teclistamab monotherapy.  CRS  occurred  in  60%  of  participants  and  was  of  low  grade  (no grade  ≥3  CRS) recovering completely. CRS started early with a median of 29 hours after the last treatment dose with  fever  most  frequently  after  the  first  or  second  step-up  dose  during  the  cycle  1.  The  most frequently needed supportive measures included tocilizumab and paracetamol in 29% and 42% of the participants, respectively.

## Neurological Adverse Events and ICANS

The  incidence  of  peripheral  neuropathy  of  any  grade  was  less  common  in  the  Tec-Dara  group compared to the control group (16.3% vs. 28.3%) and was similar with that reported priorly for teclistamab monotherapy. In the Tec-Dara group, 49 participants (17.3%) suffered from neurotoxicity events of low grade with short duration. Of these events, the most frequently reported event was headache (8.8%). Among the participants treated with the combination of teclistamab and daratumumab, the frequency of ICANS was low (3 participants, 1.1%). Two participants suffered from grade 1 events and one from grade 4 event. All participants recovered.

## Cytopenic AEs and haemorrhage

Cytopenic TEAEs were frequently reported in both treatment groups and majority of them were grade 3 or 4 events (in TecDara 82.3%, in control group 86.9%). New onset of grade ≥ 3 cytopenic TEAEs in the Tec-Dara group occurred most commonly during the first 6 months of treatment and decreased over  time,  which  may,  at  least  partly,  be  associated  with  achieving  treatment  response  with decreasing plasma cell involvement in the bone marrow. In case of cytopenias, the SmPC includes

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

adequate recommendations for dose modifications and supportive care strategies. Growth factor use is allowed, although not during active CRS which is also stated in the SmPC.

Haemorrhagic events of any grade were reported with similar frequencies in both treatment groups (12.7% in Tec-Dara group and 14.5% in controls). Even though grade 3 or 4 thrombocytopenia occurred  in  approximately  a  fifth  of  the  participants  in  both  treatment  groups,  grade  3  or  4 haemorrhagic events were infrequent, and no fatal bleedings were reported. The SmPC section 4.2 recommends different withhold levels of teclistamab treatment in case of thrombocytopenia: platelet count less than 25 000/µL in teclistamab monotherapy and platelet count less than 50 000/µL in combination  therapy  of  teclistamab  and  daratumumab.  The  different  thresholds  reflect  the differences in the protocol recommendations in MajesTEC-1 and MajesTEC-3 studies. As thrombocytopenia is a common ADR associated with daratumumab, a more conservative platelet threshold for teclistamab withhold was implemented in the MajesTEC-3 study.

## Infections

The participants in the Tec-Dara group experienced infections more frequently than the participants in the control group (96.5% vs. 84.1%, respectively). Among pathogen-specified infections, viral infections were observed in 76% of the participants in the Tec-Dara group with COVID-19 being the most  common  viral  infection  (43.8%),  which  probably  reflects  active  diagnostic  evaluations performed due to respiratory symptoms during and shortly after the COVID-19 pandemia. Of note, the majority of the participants (&gt;80%) in both groups had received the first COVID-19 vaccine before the first study dose. Septicaemias and opportunistic infections (CMV, herpes) occurred slightly more  frequently  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  than  in  the  controls  (10%  vs.  4.4%  and  9%  vs.  3%, respectively). The low frequency of herpes viral infections likely results from the vast majority of subjects using prophylactic antiviral medication.

Among the grade ≥3 pathogen unspecified infections, lower respiratory tract and lung infections (18.7%)  were  the  most  frequently  reported  infections  in  the  TecDara  group.  Among  grade  ≥3 pathogen-specified infections, viral infections were observed in 83 participants (29.3%) with COVID19 pneumonia being the most common grade ≥3 viral infection (11.3%). The incidence of grade &gt;3 COVID-19 (including COVID-19 pneumonia) was higher in the Tec-Dara group than in the control group (15.2% vs. 4.5%, respectively), whereas no difference in the incidences of grade &gt;3 nonCOVID-19  pneumonias  between  the  groups  could  be  detected.  Grade  &gt;3  bacterial  and  fungal infections were less frequent (19.1% and 5.3%, respectively) than viral infections. As among all infectious TEAEs, newonset grade ≥3 infections occurred most commonly during the first 6 months of treatment, that is not unexpected in the patients suffering from active multiple myeloma.

Despite  no  difference  in  the  incidences  of  neutropenia  between  the  groups  was  detected,  the frequencies of grade &gt;3 infections were higher in the Tec-Dara group than in the controls (54.1 vs. 43.4%, respectively). The higher incidences of hypogammaglobulinemia and lymphopenia in the TecDara group may, at least partly, explain the higher susceptibility of the participants to bacterial and viral  infections  in  the  combination  treatment  of  teclistamab  with  daratumumab.  Additionally, infections are associated with the treatment with bispecific T-cell engagers, particularly with BCMAtargeted agents, most probably due to ongoing T-cell activation, T-cell exhaustion, or treatmentinduced depletion of some T-cell populations.

Due  to  the  high  incidence  of  infections,  the  MAH  has  added  recommendations  for  antimicrobial prophylaxis and vaccinations in the SmPC section 4.4 Warnings and precautions for use.

## Hepatotoxicity

Hepatotoxicity events, primarily elevated liver enzymes, were more common in the Tec-Dara group

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

than in the control group (30.4% vs. 12.8%). The incidence of grade &gt;3 hepatotoxicity events was low.  One  grade  5  event  was  reported  in  each  treatment  group  (fulminant  hepatitis  with  severe COVID-19 infection in the Tec-Dara group and hepatic failure of unknown cause in the control group). No cases met Hy's law criteria.

## Second primary malignancies

Second primary malignancies occurred in 12.4% of the participants in the Tec-Dara group and in 8.6% of the participants in the control group and were most commonly cutaneous/non-invasive. The incidence rates of haematologic and solid malignancies were balanced between the groups.

## Injection -site and systemic administration-related reactions

Injection site reactions of low grades were common to teclistamab (in 38.5% of the participants). In both treatment groups the incidence of systemic administration-related reactions was low.

## Immunogenicity

No apparent effect of immunogenicity on PK, clinical efficacy, or safety (including sARR and other antibody-mediated safety signals) in the Tec-Dara combination was reported.

## Safety in special populations

No notable differences in the safety profile of the Tec-Dara combination among subgroups defined by age group, sex, race, baseline renal function, and baseline hepatic function were detected.

The incidences of all grade 3 or 4 TEAEs were similar in different age groups (&lt;65 yrs, 65-&lt;75 yrs, &gt;75 yrs). Fatal TEAEs were more frequent in participants aged ≥75 years, but rates were similar between both treatment groups. No specific subgroup of participants potentially more vulnerable to grade &gt;3 infections could be identified.

In  the  SmPC  section  4.8,  the  MAH  has,  upon  request,  added  a  new  chapter  entitled  Elderly, presenting the numbers of patients in different age groups (&lt;65, 65-74, &gt;75 years) in clinical trials, and the incidences of SAEs and grade &gt;3 infections in these age groups.

## Supportive study

The RPD2 cohorts in the TriMM-2 study were provided as supportive data for safety profile because the teclistamab doses (1.5 mg/kg weekly or 3 mg/kg Q2W) are most similar to the dose schedule of the teclistamab in MajesTEC-3 study.

Of note, a fixed dose teclistamab cohort in TriMM-2 study was discontinued subsequent to an urgent safety measure on February 2023 related to the incidence of fatal infections in participants treated with fixed doses of teclistamab in combination with daratumumab. The fixed dose teclistamab cohort is not relevant to the proposed dosing.

Safety data from the Tec-Dara RP2D cohorts in the TriMM-2 study were generally consistent with those from MajesTEC-3. The incidences of treatment-emergent grade 3 or 4 TEAEs, serious TEAEs, CRS, neurotoxicity and treatment-emergent grade 3 or 4 cytopenic TEAEs reported in RPD2 cohorts could be considered similar to those reported in the MajesTEC-3.

The  provided  safety  data  of  Tec-Dara  RP2D  cohorts  in  TriMM-2  study  is  at  best  supportive.  No additional safety concerns were detected.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.5.2. Conclusions on clinical safety

The total number of subjects included in the safety database enables a reasonable characterisation of the overall safety profile and common TEAEs.

Severe infections are the key risks of the combination treatment of teclistamab and daratumumab as they were experienced by approximately half of the participants in the Tec-Dara group. However, the incidence of fatal infections was low and additional warnings and guidance on supportive treatments to minimise this risk have been added to the SmPC.

No new or unexpected safety concerns have been identified, with the exception of weight decreased which has been added to the ADRs associated with teclistamab use.

## 2.5.3. PSUR cycle

The requirements for submission of periodic safety update reports for this medicinal product are set out in the list of Union reference dates (EURD list) provided for under Article 107c(7) of Directive 2001/83/EC and any subsequent updates published on the European medicines web-portal.

## 2.6. Risk management plan

The MAH submitted an updated RMP version with this application.

The CHMP received the following PRAC Advice on the submitted Risk Management Plan:

The PRAC considered that the risk management plan version 6.1 is acceptable.

## 2.7. Update of the Product information

As a consequence of this new indication, sections 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.7, 4.8, 5.1, 5.2 and 6.6 of the SmPC have been updated. The Package Leaflet has been updated accordingly.

## 2.7.1. User consultation

A justification for not performing a full user consultation with target patient groups on the package leaflet has been submitted by the MAH and has been found acceptable for the following reasons:

- the package leaflet included in this current application has the same format as the one previously approved which was subject to a full user testing
- minimal changes have been introduced to the package leaflet, and the proposed changes reflect language and a format that is consistent with that in the currently approved leaflet

## 3. Benefit-Risk Balance

## 3.1. Therapeutic Context

## 3.1.1. Disease or condition

The MAH is submitting an extension of indication application to add a new indication of teclistamab solution  for  injection  in  combination  with  daratumumab  for  the  treatment  of  adult  patients  with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least one prior therapy.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 3.1.2. Available therapies and unmet medical need

Much progress has been made over the past decade in the understanding of myeloma disease biology and individualised treatment approaches. Several new classes of drugs have joined the traditional armamentarium (corticosteroids, alkylating agents and anthracyclines) and, along with high-dose therapy  and  autologous  haemopoietic  stem  cell  transplantation,  have  led  to  deeper  and  durable clinical  responses.  Furthermore,  CAR-T  therapies  are  now  approved  at  second  and  third  line.  In addition, several bispecifics are currently approved for patients who have received 4 or more lines of therapy.

Due  these  achievement  survival  outcomes  for  patients  with  multiple  myeloma  (MM)  have  also significantly improved over the past 15 years.

There are several treatment options available for initial treatment of MM, as well as for treatment of relapsed disease. A number of different combination regimens have been studied in clinical trials in these settings. Despite this, there remains a need for more treatment options, especially for the increasing number of patients who have received 1 or more prior lines of therapy and are exposed or refractory to standard agents like PIs, lenalidomide, and anti-CD38 antibodies.

## 3.1.3. Main clinical studies

The main evidence of efficacy submitted is derived from one, open label, phase 3 study MajesTEC-3 (64007957MMY3001) with PFS as the primary endpoint.

A total of 587 participants, previously treated with at least 1 prior line of therapy, were randomised to evaluate the efficacy and safety of teclistamab in combination with subcutaneous daratumumab (Tec-Dara)  versus  subcutaneous  daratumumab,  pomalidomide,  and  dexamethasone  (DPd)  or subcutaneous daratumumab, bortezomib, and dexamethasone (DVd) in subjects with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma.

## 3.2. Favourable effects

PFS assessed by IRC showed a statistically significant benefit in the Tec-Dara group compared with the DPd/DVd group as demonstrated by an HR of 0.17 (95% CI: 0.12, 0.23), p &lt;0.0001. With a median follow-up of 34.5 months, median PFS was not reached in the Tec-Dara group and was 18.1 months (95% CI 14.6, 22.8) in the DPd/DVd group.

The rate of CR or better based on IRC assessment was statistically significantly higher in the TecDara group than the control group (81.8% vs 32.1%; OR=9.56 [95% CI: 6.47, 14.14]; p&lt;0.0001).

The MRD-negative CR rate (at 10 -5 ) in the Tec-Dara group was 57.6% vs 17.1% in the control group; OR=6.54 [95% CI: 4.37, 9.77]; p&lt;0.0001.

The OS showed a statistically significant benefit of Tec-Dara with HR of 0.46 (95% CI: 0.32, 0.65); p&lt;0.0001.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 3.3. Uncertainties and limitations about favourable effects

The crossing of the KM-curve for OS at around 10 months after randomisation is of concern and was attributed primarily to fatal infections which occurred in the early stage of the study, despite the overall positive effect on OS in the ITT population. (See also uncertainties of unfavourable effects).

Since refractoriness to an anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody was an exclusion criterion, the number of participants  with  previous  exposure  to  an  anti-CD38  monoclonal  antibody  was  low.  Although daratumumab and isatuximab are currently approved and utilised already in fist line treatment, the non-anti-CD38 exposed 2L+ population is still considered relevant in European setting.

Follow  up  for  both  PFS  and  OS  is  ongoing  and  to  further  demonstrate  the  long  term  effects  of teclistamab treatment the MAH will provide updated PFS and OS data once these are available.

## 3.4. Unfavourable effects

TEAEs were reported in all participants, with at least one Grade 3 to Grade 4 event in 95.1% of the participants  in  the  Tec-Dara  group  and  in  96.6%  in  the  control  group.  These  TEAEs  were  most frequently  reported  in  the  SOCs  of  Blood  and  lymphatic  system  disorders  and  Infections  and infestations.

Treatment-emergent infections were reported in 96.5% and in 84.1% of the participants in the TecDara and control group, respectively. Grade 3 or 4 infections were reported in 54% of the participants in the Tec-Dara group compared to 43.4% of the participants in the control group.

Fatal AEs were experienced by 7.1% and 5.9% of the participants in Tec-Dara and control group, respectively. In the Tec-Dara group, thirteen out of 20 participants with a fatal AE died from infections (4.6% of the participants), of which septicaemias were most common (5 participants).

At least 1 event of any grade CRS was reported in 60% of participants treated in the Tec-Dara group. Most events occurred during the step-up stage of dosing. CRS started with a median of 29 hours after the last treatment dose with fever, and median event duration was 2 days. CRS was frequently managed  with  supportive  measures,  including  tocilizumab  in  29%  of  participants  requiring  any supportive measures. No Grade &gt;3 CRS was observed.

In  the  Tec-Dara  group,  17% of participants  experienced teclistamab-related neurotoxicity. Three grade  3  and  one  grade  4  events  were  reported;  Three  participants  experienced  ICANS:  two participants grade 2 and one participant grade 4.

Hypogammaglobulinemia (either a TEAE or a post-baseline IgG value &lt;400 mg/dL) was reported in 239 participants (84.5%) in the Tec-Dara group and in 175 participants (60.3%) in the control group.

Grade 3 or 4 hypogammaglobulinemia were reported more frequently in the Tec-Dara arm compared to control group (5.7% vs. 1.4%).

Cytopenic events were frequently reported in both treatment groups. Grade 3 to 4 neutropenia, anaemia and thrombocytopenia were reported in 75.6%, 20.5% and 19.4% of the participants in the Tec-Dara  group  and  in  78.6%,  17.2%  and  23.4%  of  the  participants  in  the  control  group, respectively.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 3.5. Uncertainties and limitations about unfavourable effects

Infections with fatal outcomes during the early stages of treatment occurred more frequently in the teclistamab treated patients. Based on the analyses performed, no distinct or clinically actionable patient subgroup at increased risk of early death specific to Tec-Dara treatment could be identified.

To mitigate this risk, in addition to the already extensive warnings and recommendations about the risk of infections, monitoring of immunoglobulin levels to maintain them at S-IgG &gt;400 mg/dl have been introduced to the SmPC. This is based on recommendations of clinical guidelines and is aligned with the MajesTEC-3 protocol even if no association between the IgG levels and fatal infections could be observed.

## 3.7. Benefit-risk assessment and discussion

## 3.7.1. Importance of favourable and unfavourable effects

Clinically  relevant  improvement  in  PFS  was  demonstrated  in  the  pivotal  study  supporting  the extension indication, MajesTEC-3. Importantly, the study also showed an OS benefit of Tec-Dara.

These results are considered clinically relevant particularly in a targeted second (or later) line patient population. Participants who have received only 1 line of prior line of antimyeloma therapy must have been  lenalidomide  refractory  which  is  in  line  with  the  current  clinical  practice;  lenalidomide  in different combinations (followed by lenalidomide maintenance) will be used until disease progression or intolerance already in first line.

Despite multiple available treatment options, there is a need for new therapies with different MoA in clinical  practice  to  overcome  resistance  to  prior  therapies.  As  such,  combining  BCMA-targeting bispecific antibody with an anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody seems to be beneficial in MM patients with 1 line of prior line of antimyeloma therapy.

The safety data are considered sufficient both in size and in terms of duration of follow-up. The overall safety profile of teclistamab in combination with daratumumab is considered similar to the known safety profile of teclistamab monotherapy reported previously. The key risks associated with the combination treatment of teclistamab and daratumumab are grade &gt;3 infections. Despite the high incidence of severe infections, the incidence of fatal infections was low.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 3.7.2. Balance of benefits and risks

The improvement in PFS and OS is considered clinically relevant and supported by all other key secondary endpoints and is considered to outweigh the known safety concerns of teclistamab which can be sufficiently managed with the proposed routine and additional risk minimisation measures.

## 3.7.3. Additional considerations on the benefit-risk balance

## Conversion from conditional to standard MA

The main uncertainties identified at the time of CMA assessment from efficacy perspective were potential selection bias in single arm trial setting, and lack of interpretable time to event endpoints. From a safety perspective, it was concluded that the absence of a concurrent control group in a heavily pre-treated patient population with multiple disease-associated complications severely limits the ability to robustly assess the safety profile of teclistamab.

To confirm the positive benefit-risk balance of teclistamab the MAH had agreed to submit results from the MajesTEC-3 study.

The current application is based on a pre-planned interim analysis. As the primary objective of the trial  was met, this analysis became the inferential analysis. Although further analyses have been planned, these will be descriptive by nature. Although the median PFS or OS was not reached, the follow-up  was  sufficient  to  adequately  characterize  efficacy,  and  demonstrate  separation  of  K-M curves and robust statistical significance. There are no key uncertainties that could be resolved by further follow-up.

MajesTEC-3 study is an RCT that enrolled nearly 600 patients. Thus, the key uncertainties related to selection bias have been adequately addressed. The study met its primary objective, and robust efficacy  results  were  supported  by  statistically  significant  secondary  endpoints.  The  selected comparators were acceptable, and replacement setting in daratumumab combinations (teclistamab vs.  bortezomib+dexamethasone or pomalidomide+dexamethasone) allows reliable assessment of efficacy.

From a safety perspective, MajesTEC-3 multiplied the size of the safety pool. Further on, no new safety concerns related to teclistamab were identified, and additional data in monotherapy setting has been provided in another extension of indication procedure, and the safety data have been added to the safety pool presented in the SmPC.

## 3.8. Conclusions

The overall B/R of Tecvayli in the new indication is positive, and the conversion into a standard marketing authorisation is also supported.

## 4. Recommendations

## Outcome

Based on the review of the submitted data, the CHMP considers the following acceptable and therefore recommends by consensus the variation to the terms of the marketing authorisation, concerning the following changes:

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

| Variation accepted   | Variation accepted                                                                 | Type              | Annexes affected     |
|----------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|----------------------|
| C.6.a                | C.6.a) Addition of a new therapeutic indication or modification of an approved one | Variation type II | I, II, IIIA and IIIB |

Extension of indication to include in combination with daratumumab treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least one prior therapy for TECVAYLI, based on interim analysis data from the pivotal study MajesTEC-3 (64007957MMY3001) listed as a Specific  Obligation  in  Annex  II  of  the  Product  Information.  This  is  an  on-going  multicentre, randomised, open-label, Phase 3 study to determine whether adding teclistamab to daratumumab (Tec-Dara) is more efficacious than adding pomalidomide/dexamethasone (DPd) or bortezomib/dexamethasone  (DVd)  to  daratumumab  in  participants  with  multiple  myeloma  who previously received 1 to 3 prior line(s) of therapy. As a consequence, sections 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.7, 4.8, 5.1, 5.2 and 6.6 of the SmPC are updated. The Package Leaflet is updated accordingly. References to the conditional MA have been removed throughout the document. Additionally, the MAH took the opportunity  to  update  the  latest  renewal  date  in  section  9  of  the  SmPC,  the  list  of  local representatives in the Package Leaflet and made editorial changes throughout. And updated RMP version 6.1 has been submitted. As part of the application, the MAH is requesting a 1-year extension of the market protection.

## Amendments to the marketing authorisation

In view of the data submitted with the variation, amendments to Annexes I, II, IIIA, IIIB and to the Risk Management Plan are recommended.

This recommendation is subject to the following amended conditions:

The following obligation has been fulfilled, and therefore it is recommended that is be deleted from the Annex II:

In order to confirm the efficacy and safety of Teclistamab indicated as monotherapy for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed and refractory multiple myeloma, who have received at least three prior therapies, including an immunomodulatory agent, a proteasome inhibitor, and an antiCD38 antibody, and have demonstrated disease progression on the last therapy, the MAH shall submit the results of study 64007957MMY3001, a Phase 3 Randomised Study Comparing Teclistamab in Combination with Daratumumab SC versus Daratumumab SC, Pomalidomide, and Dexamethasone (DPd) or Daratumumab SC, Bortezomib, and Dexamethasone (DVd) in Participants with Relapsed or Refractory Multiple Myeloma.

## Conditions or restrictions with regard to the safe and effective use of the medicinal product

## · Risk management plan (RMP)

The MAH shall perform the required pharmacovigilance activities and interventions detailed in the agreed RMP presented in Module 1.8.2 of the Marketing Authorisation and any agreed subsequent updates of the RMP.

In addition, an updated RMP should be submitted:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

At the request of the European Medicines Agency;

Whenever the risk management system is modified, especially as the result of new information being received that may lead to a significant change to the benefit/risk profile or as the result of an important (pharmacovigilance or risk minimisation) milestone being reached.

## Similarity with authorised orphan medicinal products

The CHMP by consensus is of the opinion that Tecvayli is not similar to Darzalex, Ninlaro, Abecma, Carvykti and Talvey within the meaning of Article 3 of Commission Regulation (EC) No. 847/200. See appendix 1.

## Additional market protection

Furthermore, the CHMP reviewed the data submitted by the MAH, taking into account the provisions of Article 14(11) of Regulation (EC) No 726/2004, and considers by consensus that the new therapeutic indication brings significant clinical benefit in comparison with existing therapies (see appendix 2).
